# One block per comment, opened by '=== <address> [<type>]'. Blocks are address-ordered,
# then ordered by type within an address. Loader fixup comments are excluded.
=== 00010000 [PLATE]

Pool hint from block triage: crt

=== 00010003 [EOL]

=== 00010010 [PLATE]
Advance a single map actor one behavior step.

Algorithm:
Resolves the actor record for the given index through the record accessor at 0002d210 (the same record is reachable as DAT_00069cd8 + index * 0x50, confirming a 0x50-byte stride). If bit 0 of record[5] is set the actor is inactive and the function returns immediately.

Otherwise it reads the behavior mode from the low nibble of record[0x34] and two byte parameters record[0x35] / record[0x36] (a destination pair), plus an event slot index in record[0x3d], and dispatches:
  mode 0, 1, 2, 9  -- try the movement handlers 00012c10 / 000126b0 / 000127c0 / 00012230 in a fixed order and fall back to 000120d0 when every attempt fails. Mode 9 first queries 0002dc20 for a target and, when one exists, walks toward it via 000119e0.
  mode 3           -- like mode 9, but keyed on 00012c10 succeeding first; on a resolved target it clears DAT_00069cd0.
  mode 4, 7        -- clear DAT_00069cd0 and walk toward the record[0x35]/record[0x36] destination via 000119e0. Mode 7, on arrival, plays the "Posion.saf" effect through 00026e00 when the terrain byte record[7] lies in 0x24..0x27, then sets bit 0 of record[5] to retire the actor.
  mode 5           -- event step: if the per-slot flag (&DAT_000640d8)[record[0x3d]] is still clear and 00013e10 yields a destination, walk there; on arrival read a 3-byte entry at DAT_0006013c + slot * 3 + 0x53, copy it into record[0x31]/record[0x32], call 00025d20 when the first byte is zero, set the slot flag, call 0002e910, switch the actor to mode 7 and play "Chess.wav" via 0002a1f0.
  mode 8           -- no action.
  mode 10, 11      -- the interactive/driven modes: 00013040 or 00013420, gated extra handlers 00027180 / 00013c90 when DAT_00063f8c / DAT_00063f88 reach 6, then 00012230, and finally 00012e50 or the 000126b0 / 000120d0 fallback depending on DAT_00063f78. Mode 11 additionally invokes 00010760 when the terrain byte record[7] is in 0x3c..0x3e.

After dispatch it re-reads the record, calls 0002e030 with the actor's current x/y and 1, sets bit 0x80 of record[5] to mark the actor for redraw, and calls fdps_render_view_frame.

Parameters:
  actor_index -- index into the actor record array at DAT_00069cd8 (0x50 bytes per record).
  context     -- second stack argument, passed unchanged to every movement handler.

Returns:
  Nothing.

=== 00011460 [PLATE]
Traces the battle movement cost map and returns either a direction path or the nearest reachable unit.

Algorithm:
The global at DAT_00060144 points to a movement map header: word width, word height, then width*height cells of 2 bytes each. Byte 1 of a cell holds the flood-filled step cost, 0xff meaning unreachable. Cell stride is 2 and row stride is width*2; both are computed into locals up front.

mode == 2: scans the unit table (count in DAT_00060150, records fetched by index through FUN_0002d210). Units are filtered by side: when arg4 is zero only units with record byte 6 == 0 are considered, otherwise only units with byte 6 != 0. Units with bit 0 of record byte 5 set are skipped. For each surviving unit the cost cell at (unit.x, unit.y) is read and the minimum is tracked. If no unit was found the function returns -1, otherwise it stores the winning unit's x and y into out_path[0] and out_path[1] and returns the minimum cost.

mode == 0 or 1: starts at (arg4, arg5) and repeatedly steps to the neighbouring cell with the lowest cost, recording one direction code per step into a local buffer: 0 = y-1, 1 = x+1, 2 = y+1, 3 = x-1, 4 = no move. Edge neighbours are skipped at the map borders. If the starting cell is already 0xff the function returns -1 immediately. Mode 0 takes a neighbour only on a strictly lower cost, which makes runs of identical cost keep the current heading. Mode 1 also accepts an equal cost, but only when the previously recorded step used a different direction code, which makes ties alternate direction instead of running straight. The walk stops once the current tile equals (arg1, arg2). The recorded direction codes are then copied into out_path in reverse order and the step count is returned.

Parameters:
arg1 goal_x   - x of the tile at which the walk terminates (unused in mode 2)
arg2 goal_y   - y of the tile at which the walk terminates (unused in mode 2)
arg3 out_path - byte buffer receiving the reversed direction codes, or the found unit's x,y in mode 2
arg4 start_x  - x of the tile the walk starts from; in mode 2 this is the side filter instead
arg5 start_y  - y of the tile the walk starts from (unused in mode 2)
arg6 mode     - 0 straight-preferring trace, 1 turn-preferring trace, 2 nearest-unit query

Returns:
Number of direction codes written in modes 0 and 1, the minimum cost found in mode 2, or -1 when the start tile is unreachable or no unit matched.

=== 000119d9 [EOL]

=== 000119e0 [PLATE]
Battle movement: walk a unit toward a requested destination tile.

Algorithm:
1. Resolve the unit record for unit_index via FUN_0002d210, and cache from it: field 0x00 = tile x, field 0x01 = tile y, field 0x20 = movement points, field 0x3b = a movement/terrain cost selector.
2. FUN_00018b70(move_points + 1) yields a per-unit movement context; malloc(100) is the step buffer (one direction code per step) and malloc(0x800) is the reachable-tile list (x,y byte pairs).
3. Build the movement/cost map (FUN_00010b90(map_mode) then FUN_00010de0(ctx, x, y, cost_selector)) and try to path from the unit to (dest_x, dest_y) with FUN_00011460(x, y, step_buf, dest_x, dest_y, 0). It returns the step count, or -1 when no path exists.
4. If that fails, retry the path with the relaxed flag 1. If the relaxed path exists, replay it step by step (direction code 0 = y+1, 1 = x-1, 2 = y-1, else x+1), probing each tile with FUN_0002ba00(x, y) and reading the occupancy result at 0x00069d0c. The last tile whose probe is not 0xff becomes the new destination, i.e. the request is retargeted to the furthest usable tile along the blocked route.
5. Rebuild the movement map, call FUN_000118f0(unit_index, map_mode) to flood the unit's reach, and FUN_00011da0(tile_buf) to collect the reachable tiles and their count.
6. Scan the reachable tiles and keep the one minimising Manhattan distance |tx - dest_x| + |ty - dest_y| to the destination, breaking ties on the smaller ||dx| - |dy|| (the more diagonal-balanced tile).
7. Path to that chosen tile with FUN_00011460(..., 0). If the step count is non-zero, FUN_0002d2d0(unit_index, step_buf, step_count) performs the actual move and the result is 1; otherwise 0.
8. Free both buffers and return.

Parameters:
  dest_x     [ebp+0x14] requested destination tile x; reassigned internally when the route is retargeted
  dest_y     [ebp+0x18] requested destination tile y; likewise reassigned
  unit_index [ebp+0x1c] index of the acting unit, resolved to its record by FUN_0002d210
  map_mode   [ebp+0x20] selector handed to the movement-map builder FUN_00010b90 and to FUN_000118f0

Returns:
  1 if a move of at least one step was executed, 0 if the unit stays where it is.

=== 00013fd0 [PLATE]
Draws one SAF tilemap layer.

Algorithm:
Takes a 0x24-byte draw context whose field 0x14 holds the base address of a loaded SAF file and whose field 0x18 holds the tilemap index. Reads the section 1 (tilemap) descriptor from the SAF header: item count at 0x16, offset-table start at 0x18. Returns immediately if the index is negative or not less than the item count. Otherwise resolves the tilemap record via saf + *(u32 *)(saf + saf[0x18] + index * 4) and reads its i16 column count and i16 row count, then advances past the 4-byte tilemap header to the cell array. Copies the whole 0x24-byte context onto the stack as a working copy (memcpy at 0x3d514) and iterates rows outer, columns inner. At the start of each row the working copy's x (offset 0x0c) is reset from the caller's x; for each cell the signed i16 tile index is stored into the working copy's index field (offset 0x18), the per-cell tile drawer at 0x13ed0 is called with the working copy, x is advanced by the SAF cell width (header 0x07) and after the row y (offset 0x10) is advanced by the SAF cell height (header 0x09). Cell indices are read signed while the caller's tilemap index is unsigned; the per-cell drawer performs its own range check.

Parameters:
  ctx - pointer to the 0x24-byte SAF draw context: 0x0c = x, 0x10 = y, 0x14 = SAF base pointer, 0x18 = tilemap index on entry (reused as tile index for each cell).

Returns:
  Nothing. Draws nothing when the tilemap index is out of range.

=== 000140d6 [EOL]
Alignment padding: Watcom multi-byte NOPs (lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0]) to 16-byte boundary

=== 0001413f [EOL]

=== 00014140 [PLATE]
Draws one composite sprite entry: a bank-indexed group of part records blitted at offsets from a common origin.

Algorithm:
1. Read the bank pointer from req+0x14 and the entry index from req+0x18. The bank header holds an unsigned 16-bit entry count at bank+0x0C and an (unaligned) 32-bit offset-table displacement at bank+0x0E. If the index is negative or >= the count, the lookup yields NULL and the function returns without drawing.
2. Otherwise entry = bank + *(int32 *)(bank + *(int32 *)(bank+0x0E) + index*4), i.e. table entries are bank-relative offsets.
3. Copy the caller's 0x24-byte draw request onto the stack as a scratch request, so per-part edits never touch the caller's copy.
4. Walk the part records, which start at entry+0x0A and are 13 bytes each; the signed 16-bit count is at entry+0x08. Each record holds: u16 sub-sprite id at +0, s16 dx at +2, s16 dy at +4, u8 mode at +6, s16 level at +7 (unaligned).
5. For each part, when req+0x20 in the caller's request is zero the record's mode byte selects the blend setup: mode 0 clears the scratch request's mode field to 0; mode 1 stores 0x10 - level into a small parameter block built from the two runtime tables at 0x000653F0 and 0x000643F0, sets the scratch mode field to 9, and points the scratch request at that block. Any other mode leaves the previous setup in place.
6. Set the scratch request's X to req+0x0C + dx, its Y to req+0x10 + dy, its sprite id to the record's u16, and call the per-part blitter at 0x00013FD0 with the scratch request.
7. After the loop, if the flag argument is non-zero, call 0x000142D0 with the bank pointer and the entry's leading u16.

Parameters:
  req  - pointer to a 0x24-byte draw request: X at +0x0C, Y at +0x10, sprite bank pointer at +0x14, entry index at +0x18, blend parameter block pointer at +0x1C, blend mode at +0x20.
  flag - when non-zero, run the post-draw call at 0x000142D0 for this entry.

Returns:
  Nothing. Silently does nothing when the entry index is out of range.

=== 000142cc [EOL]
4-byte Watcom alignment filler (lea eax,[eax+0]) between fdps_draw_composite_sprite and fdps_sfx_play

=== 000142d0 [PLATE]
Algorithm: Returns immediately unless both sound-enable flags (DAT_00069d71, DAT_00069d70) are set and the entry index is within [0, bank->entry_count) where entry_count is the u16 at bank+0x2a. Locates the entry through the dword offset table at bank+0x2c: entry = bank + *(int*)(bank + *(int*)(bank+0x2c) + index*4), which points at an 8-byte header followed by raw PCM data. Scans the 8-slot sample-handle array at 0x00069d30 for the first handle whose AIL sample status is not 4 (playing); if all eight are busy the request is dropped silently. On the chosen handle it calls the Miles sequence init sample, set sample address (entry+8, length = *(int*)(entry+4)), set sample type (format code, flags 2), set loop count 1, set volume 0x28, set playback rate (u16 at entry+2), start sample. The format code is derived from the first two header bytes, channels at +0 and bits at +1: (1,8)->0, (1,16)->1, (2,8)->2, anything else->3, matching the Miles DIG_F_MONO_8 / MONO_16 / STEREO_8 / STEREO_16 enumeration.
Parameters: bank - base pointer of a loaded sound bank whose header holds the entry count at +0x2a and the entry offset table pointer at +0x2c; index - zero-based entry index within that bank.
Returns: nothing; failure to validate, disabled sound, or no free sample handle all return silently.

=== 000144db [EOL]

=== 00014544 [EOL]

=== 00014550 [PLATE]
Advance a SAF animation playback cursor by one tick.

Algorithm:
The cursor is a 3-dword state block: [0] current frame index, [1] tick counter inside the current frame, [2] pointer to the loaded SAF image. When mode == 1 the call is a reset: both counters are zeroed and 0 is returned without touching the SAF. Otherwise the SAF header is sanity-checked by comparing the magic bytes 'S', 'A' and 'F' at offsets 0, 1 and 2 (note the original code accepts the file if ANY one of the three matches, not all three), and the frame count is taken from the u16 at header offset 0x0C; a zero frame count makes the function return -1. The tick counter is then incremented and the current frame record is located as saf + *(i32 *)(saf + *(i32 *)(saf + 0x0E) + index * 4), with the index bounds-checked against the frame count (an out-of-range index yields a null frame pointer). The frame's hold time is the i16 at frame offset 0x02; while the tick counter stays below it the frame is held. Once the counter reaches the hold time it is cleared and the frame index is incremented. If the index then reaches the frame count the animation has completed one pass: the tick counter is cleared and, when mode == 0, the index wraps to 0 to loop, otherwise it is clamped to the last frame so playback stops on the final frame; the function returns 1 in that case.

Parameters:
cursor - pointer to the 3-dword playback state block described above.
mode - 1 resets the cursor, 0 loops back to the first frame at the end, any other value holds on the last frame.

Returns:
1 when the animation just passed its last frame, 0 while it is still playing or after a reset, -1 when the SAF data is unusable (bad magic or zero frame count).

=== 00014ab0 [PLATE]
Battle system menu (modal loop).

Algorithm:
Copies two 16-byte tables out of the read-only island at 0x146e0 and 0x146f0 into locals: four menu entry codes {0x16, 0x0b, 0x0c, 0x13} and a matching four-entry state array cleared to zero. Clears the two menu-active globals DAT_00069cd0 and DAT_00060159 and enters the loop.

Each iteration draws the menu (FUN_00016130), reads a selection (FUN_00016200, which updates the selection index and returns -1 when the player backs out), then runs the close animation as a countdown 0x17, 0x13, 0x0f, 0x0b, 0x07, 0x03 through FUN_00015860 and calls FUN_0002beb0.

A -1 result sets both menu-active globals to 1 and returns 0. Otherwise the selection index picks a branch:
  index 0: calls FUN_00014ea0; any result other than -1 sets both globals to 1 and is returned to the caller as the menu result, otherwise the loop continues.
  index 1: darkens/prepares via FUN_000205b0(0), shows panel 0xaa44a with code 0x1ea, then calls FUN_00017990. On a zero result it shows panel 0xabc0a code 0x1eb, waits 300 ticks, dismisses (FUN_00020820), recomputes the cursor tile from DAT_00069cd4/24 and DAT_00069ccc/24, clears both menu-active globals, and walks every unit 0..DAT_00060150-1 (FUN_0002d210): for a unit whose byte 5 has none of mask 0x85 set, whose byte 6 equals 2 and whose byte 0x26 is zero, it centres on the unit tile (FUN_0002d7c0), sets DAT_00069d90 to 0xff, calls FUN_000119e0, dispatches through the handler table at 0x000601c4 when DAT_00069d90 changed, and finishes the unit with FUN_000119b0. It then redraws (FUN_0001e3f0), sets both globals to 1, calls FUN_0002db50 and returns 0. A non-zero result shows panel 0xabc0a code 0x1ec, waits and returns to the loop.
  index 2: calls FUN_000183d0 and stays in the loop.
  index 3: same shape as index 1 with codes 0x1ed / 0x1ee / 0x1ef and panel 0xad3ca; on success it clears DAT_00060159, redraws with FUN_0001e3f0, restores the flag and returns 0.

Parameters:
None. The prologue pushes EBX/ESI/EDI/EBP and builds a 0x48-byte frame but never reads [EBP+0x14] or beyond, so the function takes no arguments; the parameters shown by the decompiler are invented.

Returns:
int. 0 when the menu is dismissed or when an entry completed its action and the battle screen was restored; otherwise the non-negative command code produced by FUN_00014ea0.

=== 00014e99 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte NOP (lea eax,[eax+0x0]) + 0x90, aligns next function entry to 0x10.

=== 00014ea0 [PLATE]
In-battle system submenu: entry action, save, load, quit.

Algorithm:
Builds two parallel 4-entry stack arrays. The id array is copied from the
constant table at 0x00014700 and holds {0x17, 9, 10, 8}; the state array is
copied from the zero table at 0x00014710 and is then patched:
  - state[2] (load) is set to 1 when the CRT file-existence check at 0x00042438
    reports that "FDE.SAV" (0x00060118) is present.
  - state[1] (save) is set to 1 when any of the DAT_00060150 unit records
    obtained through FUN_0002d210(index) has flags byte +0x05 with bit 0 clear
    and bit 7 set.
Then loops: FUN_00016130 draws the menu, FUN_00016200 runs the selection and
returns the chosen index through an out parameter, FUN_000161b0 tears the menu
down, and fdps_render_view_frame repaints the battlefield. A return of -1 from
FUN_00016200 aborts the whole submenu with -1.
Selection handling:
  0 - calls FUN_00017ca0 and returns 0.
  1 - save. Shows message 0x1F3, asks for confirmation via FUN_00017990. On
      confirmation allocates a 0x59CB byte image, seeds it from the existing
      "FDE.SAV" opened "rb" (or, when no file exists, writes 0xFF slot markers
      at +0x3B2B, +0x4553, +0x4F7B, +0x59A3), copies 0x8A3 bytes from
      DAT_0006013C, 0xA00 bytes from DAT_00064108 at +0x8A3, DAT_00060150*0x50
      bytes from DAT_00069CD8 at +0x12A3 and 0x20 bytes from 0x000640D8 at
      +0x30A3, fills the descriptor at +0x30C3 (unit count, three coordinate
      globals divided by 0x18, chapter/flag globals), stores the FUN_00056898
      checksum at +0x59C7, scrambles with FUN_000568B7, writes the image back
      through "wb", frees it, reports 0x203, and returns 0.
  2 - load. Shows message 0x205, asks for confirmation, and on confirmation
      reports 0x206 and calls fdps_load_savegame; returns 0.
  3 - quit. Shows message 0x1F0, asks for confirmation, and on confirmation
      reports 0x1F1, sets the quit flag DAT_000643EB to 1 and returns 1.
A declined confirmation falls through to the top of the loop and redisplays the
menu.

Parameters:
None.

Returns:
-1 when the menu was aborted, 1 when the player confirmed quit (DAT_000643EB
has been set), 0 after any other selection completed.

=== 0001546f [EOL]
Single 0x90 NOP alignment pad between fdps_battle_system_submenu and fdps_battle_unit_turn.

=== 00015470 [PLATE]
Interactive turn of a single player-controlled battle unit.

Algorithm:
Loads the unit record for unit_index (helper at 0002d210; records are 0x50 bytes in the array based at 00069cd8). Field 0x3b selects a graphics resource that is acquired once up front (0003d375) and released once at the end (0003d478); field 0x20 is the movement allowance; field 0x08 is the class code; fields 0x00/0x01 are the tile X/Y. Two 16-byte command-menu templates are copied from rodata into locals: the command id list {3,2,1,0} and a per-entry state array {0,0,0,0}. Sets the global at 0006015c to 0x14 and then loops until the turn is finished.

Each pass: refreshes the map, resets the pending-action selector at 00069d90 to 0xff, computes the reachable set for (movement allowance + 1) via 00018b70, converts the current cursor pixel position (00069cd4 / 00069ccc) to tile coordinates by dividing by the 24-pixel tile size, paints the movement range (00010de0) and waits for a map selection (0002b4f0). A -1 selection restores the cursor to the origin tile and ends the turn. Otherwise the destination picker at 00011460 runs; its result -1 restarts the loop (move cancelled), 0 means the unit stays put, any other value is a path to walk. For a real move the class code is checked (values 7 and 3 leave menu entry 1 disabled, all others enable it), the walk is played back by 0002d2d0 and the command menu at 00015d00 is opened with the moved flag set; for a stationary unit the same menu is opened with the flag clear. A -1 from the menu is a cancel: when the global at 00060004 is zero the unit is written back to its origin tile and the loop repeats, otherwise the turn is committed. Any other menu result commits the turn.

On exit sets 00069cd0, releases the graphics resource, and if 00069d90 is no longer 0xff calls the handler at index 00069d90 of the function-pointer table at 000601c4 with unit_index, then calls the handler at index 00069cf4 of the table at 0006028c.

Parameters:
  unit_index - index of the acting unit within the battle unit array.

Returns:
  Nothing. The chosen action is carried out through the dispatch tables at 000601c4 and 0006028c.

=== 0001585b [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] + mov ecx,ecx, aligning next function entry to 0x15860

=== 00015b86 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte and 4-byte lea-form NOPs aligning FUN_00015b90 to 16 bytes.

=== 00015bdf [EOL]
Single-byte 0x90 NOP alignment filler between FUN_00015b90 and FUN_00015be0.

=== 00015cf2 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov eax,eax, aligning next function to 0x10.

=== 00015d00 [PLATE]
Battle action command menu for a single unit.

Algorithm:
Outer do/while loop, re-entered whenever the chosen command aborts back to the menu.
Each pass:
  1. Clears cmd_disabled[0] and the global at 0x00069cec, then probes availability:
     - FUN_00025140(unit, 0) == -1  -> cmd_disabled[0] = 1 (command 0 unusable);
       otherwise FUN_00025200/FUN_00018b40 fetch a descriptor whose bytes +0x0b and
       +0x0c are range values fed, together with the cursor tile derived from the
       globals 0x00069cd4 and 0x00069ccc divided by the 24-pixel tile size, into
       FUN_00011e50; a zero result also sets cmd_disabled[0] = 1. FUN_00010b20
       releases whatever FUN_00011e50 built.
     - FUN_00025240(unit) == 0 -> cmd_disabled[2] = 1.
     - FUN_00027840(unit, 0) == 0, or byte +0x27 of the record from FUN_0002d210(unit)
       is non-zero -> cmd_disabled[1] = 1.
  2. Scans cmd_disabled[0..3] for the first zero entry and uses it as the initial
     highlighted index (-1 when every command is disabled).
  3. FUN_00016130 draws the menu, FUN_00016200 runs the selection loop and writes the
     chosen index back through a pointer, FUN_000161b0 erases it, then
     fdps_render_view_frame repaints. A selection result of -1 means cancel: the
     function returns the pending result value (-1, or 1 when command 2 previously
     reported code 2).
  4. Dispatch on the chosen index:
     index 0: saves the cursor position, allocates a 100-byte work block
       (FUN_0003d375), rebuilds the reachable/target set via FUN_00011e50, sets the
       global 0x00069cd0 to 1 and runs target selection FUN_0002b4f0, then frees the
       block. On cancel the cursor is restored with fdps_map_cursor_move_to and the
       menu is shown again. On success it resolves the action:
       fdps_battle_find_unit_at_cursor, FUN_0001c2e0, FUN_00018d60, FUN_00026180,
       FUN_0001d6c0, scales the 0x00069cec accumulator by 15/10, then FUN_0001dd30 and
       FUN_0001d990 apply the result and FUN_000119b0 finishes the turn; returns 1.
     index 1: FUN_00027c20(unit); on success FUN_000119b0 ends the turn, the byte flag
       at 0x00060159 is set to 1 and 1 is returned, otherwise the menu is shown again.
     index 2: FUN_000252b0(unit); result 2 latches the pending return value to 1 and
       re-shows the menu, any other non-(-1) result clears 0x00069cec and returns 1.
     any other index: when flag == 0 calls FUN_000120d0(unit) first, then FUN_000184f0
       and FUN_000119b0, and returns 1.

Parameters:
  unit         - pointer to the acting unit record; passed to every availability probe
                 and command handler.
  menu_ctx     - context value handed to the menu draw, select and erase helpers.
  cmd_disabled - caller-supplied int[4] scratch array; this function fills it with the
                 per-command disabled flags (0 = selectable, 1 = greyed out).
  flag         - when zero, the fall-through command runs the extra FUN_000120d0 step.

Returns:
  1 when a command was carried out and the unit's action is complete.
  -1 when the menu was cancelled with nothing done (caller returns to unit selection);
  1 is returned instead if command 2 had earlier reported result code 2.

=== 000160dd [EOL]
Alignment padding: 3-byte lea eax,[eax+0] NOP form aligning next function to 4 bytes.

=== 000161ac [EOL]
Alignment padding: 4-byte NOP form lea eax,[eax+eiz*1+0x0] aligning 000161b0 to 16

=== 00016200 [PLATE]
Modal four-way menu cursor input loop.

Algorithm:
Loops until a terminating key arrives. Each iteration reads one scan code
(masked to a byte) from the low-level keyboard reader at 0x000567be and
dispatches on it:
  0x01 (Esc) or 0x53      -> result = -1 (cancel), loop ends
  0x1c (Enter) or 0x39 (Space) -> result = 1 (confirm), loop ends
  0x48 (Up)    -> if dir_blocked[0] == 0 then *cursor_dir = 0
  0x4b (Left)  -> if dir_blocked[1] == 0 then *cursor_dir = 1
  0x4d (Right) -> if dir_blocked[2] == 0 then *cursor_dir = 2
  0x50 (Down)  -> if dir_blocked[3] == 0 then *cursor_dir = 3
A non-zero entry in dir_blocked suppresses movement in that direction, so the
cursor keeps its previous value. Any other scan code falls through unhandled.
After dispatch every iteration unconditionally calls 0x0002eab0 and 0x00015be0
(frame pacing / presentation), then calls 0x00015860(ctx, dir_blocked, 0x18,
*cursor_dir) to repaint the cursor, then repeats.

Parameters:
  ctx          - opaque context/handle forwarded unchanged as the first
                 argument of the redraw call at 0x00015860.
  dir_blocked  - pointer to four ints indexed by direction (0 up, 1 left,
                 2 right, 3 down); zero means that direction is selectable.
                 Also forwarded to the redraw call.
  cursor_dir   - in/out pointer to the currently selected direction index;
                 updated in place on an accepted arrow key.

Returns:
  1  on confirm (Enter or Space)
  -1 on cancel (Esc or scan code 0x53)
The function never returns while the loop result is still 0.

=== 000162fd [EOL]
3-byte alignment padding (lea eax,[eax+0]) before fdps_draw_unit_status_panel

=== 00016300 [PLATE]
Draws the body of the unit status panel for one unit.

Algorithm:
1. If the global mode flag at 0x00060070 is set, latch the caller-supplied unit index into 0x00063fb0.
2. Resolve the unit record via the accessor at 0x0002d210(unit_index) and copy the fields it will print into locals: current/max HP (record+0x40 / +0x42), current/max MP (record+0x44 / +0x46), a two-digit value at +0x21, four three-digit stats at +0x48, +0x4a, +0x4c, +0x4e, a three-digit value at +0x3e, a two-digit value at +0x3b, and byte fields +0x07, +0x08, +0x1f, +0x20 used as sprite indices. The byte at +0x3c is displayed as 1000 when it holds the sentinel 0xff.
3. Pick the portrait entry from the table based at 0x00060138 (stride 0x30, first dword of the entry is an offset relative to the table base). The index is record+0x02 in the normal path and the caller-supplied unit index when 0x00060070 is set. Blit that 0x18 x 0x18 image at destination offset 0xd21.
4. Print every numeric field with the digit renderer at 0x00017530 (destination, pitch 0x140, value, digit count, 0). Before each print, the global colour selector 0x0006000c is set to 3 when the current value differs from its maximum, to 1 when the corresponding status byte (record+0x22, +0x23, +0x24) is non-zero, and back to 0 otherwise.
5. Draw the HP gauge at offset 0x9396 and the MP gauge at offset 0xab56 with the bar renderer at 0x00017740; the filled length is 0 when the maximum is not positive, otherwise ceil(current * 0x75 / maximum).
6. Draw the field at record+0x07 with the renderer at 0x000177d0 at offset 0x513, then blit three icons through 0x0001ff60 from the sheet in 0x000643c4 using sprite indices record+0x08 plus 1, record+0x20 plus 0xa1 and record+0x1f plus 0x97, at destination offsets 0x709, 0x1d80 and 0x1dc7.

All destination offsets are byte offsets into a buffer whose pitch is 0x140 (320) pixels, so they encode fixed y*320+x positions inside the panel.

Parameters:
  unit_index - index of the unit whose record is fetched and drawn.
  dest - base address of the destination pixel buffer (320-byte pitch).

Returns:
  Nothing.

=== 00016835 [EOL]
Watcom 16-byte alignment padding between fdps_draw_unit_status_panel and fdps_load_status_cel_image.

=== 00016840 [PLATE]
Loads the Status.cel image out of MISC.VFS into a new 320x200 offscreen buffer.

Algorithm:
  1. fdps_vfs_open("MISC.VFS" @ 0x60128) -> archive handle.
  2. fdps_vfs_load_file("Status.cel" @ 0x61590, handle) -> heap block holding the raw CEL file.
  3. malloc(0xfa00 = 64000) -> destination image buffer (320*200, one byte per pixel).
  4. free(archive handle) - the directory handle is no longer needed once the entry is read.
  5. Pixel payload starts at cel_base + *(int *)(cel_base + 0x0f); that pointer plus the destination
     buffer are handed to the CEL decoder at 0x568db with (320, 200, 320, 0, 0), i.e. full frame,
     destination pitch 320, drawn at origin.
  6. free(raw CEL block) and return the decoded image buffer.

Parameters:
  none.

Returns:
  void * - malloc'd 64000 byte 320x200 8bpp image containing the decoded Status.cel.
  The caller owns the block and must free it.

=== 000168d9 [EOL]

=== 00016a95 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 11-byte multi-byte NOP run aligning the next function entry to 16.

=== 00016aa0 [PLATE]
Battle unit information window.

Algorithm:
1. Resolves the unit record for the incoming unit id via FUN_0002d210 and reads the class byte at offset 7. If the class is in the range 0x24..0x27 the function returns immediately without touching the screen, so those unit kinds have no information window.
2. Acquires the drawing context via FUN_00016840, saves the global flag at 0x00060159 into 0x00063fb8 and clears it, and calls FUN_0002beb0 when the flag at 0x00060070 is clear.
3. Plays the sound effect named by the string at 0x0006159c ("OpWin1.wav", the window-open cue).
4. Allocates a 64000-byte buffer and copies the whole 320x200 VGA frame from 0x000a0000 into it, then allocates a second 0x57e3-byte buffer (151*149) for the window rectangle, and saves the current contents of the window rectangle out of the off-screen page (context + 0x3b58, i.e. screen position x=152, y=47, pitch 320).
5. Renders the panel into the off-screen page: frame/background via FUN_00016300 and FUN_00024ea0, nine content rows via FUN_00016e80 called once per row index 0..8, then FUN_000170c0 with the unit field at offset 2.
6. Queries the page content with FUN_00027840 into a 40-byte local descriptor. When the returned count is non-zero the window is animated open by FUN_0002fe40 with step 2,4,6,8,10, each step waiting on one full vertical retrace read from VGA status port 0x3da; the reverse loop (11 down to 1, step -2) animates it closed.
7. When the returned count exceeds 8 a second page is drawn (FUN_000276f0 with page index 8) and cross-faded in by FUN_00030230 in 8 steps (1..15 step 2), again one retrace per step, before the closing animation.
8. Restores state via FUN_000168e0, frees the three buffers, and calls FUN_000567b3.

Parameters:
  unit_id - identifier of the unit whose information window is shown; passed straight to the unit record lookup and to the page/text routines.

Returns:
  Nothing. The screen and the saved global flag are restored before returning.

=== 00016e71 [EOL]
Alignment padding: three multi-byte LEA NOP forms filling 15 bytes to the next function entry at 00016e80.

=== 00016e80 [PLATE]
Renders one frame of the four-panel status window slide-in animation.

Algorithm:
1. Copies four 9-entry int tables (0x14788, 0x147ac, 0x147d0, 0x147f4) onto the stack; each holds the per-step offset of one panel. Values converge on 15 / 108 / 148 / 0 and overshoot by one pixel at steps 6-7, giving the window a small bounce as it settles.
2. Allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) off-screen buffer and copies the background image argument into it.
3. Blits four rectangles of the composed window image into that buffer with fdps_blit_rect, each clipped to the current step offset so it appears to slide in from its own edge:
   - upper-left  panel, final rows 0..107,   cols 15..147,  grows leftward from column 148
   - lower-left  panel, final rows 108..199, cols 15..147,  grows upward from the bottom
   - lower-right panel, final rows 44..199,  cols 148..305, grows leftward, width capped at 158
   - upper-right panel, final rows 0..43,    cols 148..305, grows upward from row 44
4. Calls the 0x3d370 thunk with 15, then polls the VGA input status register at port 0x3DA until vertical retrace begins and ends.
5. Copies the 64000-byte buffer to 0xA0000 and frees it.

Parameters:
  background   pointer to a 320x200 8bpp image used as the frame backdrop
  window_image pointer to a 320x200 8bpp image holding the assembled window graphics
  step         animation step index, 0..8, used to index the four offset tables

Returns:
  nothing.

=== 000170bc [EOL]
4-byte alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] (8d 44 20 00)

=== 000170c0 [PLATE]
Frame-paced input wait loop for the unit status window.

Algorithm:
Copies a 15-entry sprite Y-position table from 0x14818 (15, 30, 70, 105, 140, 170, 150, 130, 150, 170, 160, 165, 165, 160, 170 -- a drop from the top of the screen followed by two bounces) into a local array, and sets the idle-animation frame counter to -1 (inactive). When the flag argument is non-zero it samples the CRT helper at 0x42cf8 and, when the value is a multiple of 200, arms the idle animation by setting the frame counter to 0.

It then loops: poll the input source at 0x178f0; a returned code greater than 0x7f means nothing selected yet, so a frame is drawn. A frame is skipped entirely unless the global tick at 0x69d64 differs from the last-drawn tick at 0x63fc0.

Per frame: allocate a 0x15180-byte 360x240 backing buffer and a 0xfa00-byte 320x200 work buffer, zero the work buffer, capture the current screen into the backing buffer (from 0xa0504 when the mode flag at 0x60070 is set, otherwise via the helper at 0x2bf60), copy it back into the work buffer, then free the backing buffer. The walk-cycle frame index is (tick at 0x63fc0 % 16) / 4, with 3 folded back to 1, giving the 0,1,2,1 cycle; it selects one of three sprite pointers inside the 0x30-byte per-unit record at the table based at 0x60138, whose four 0x0c-byte groups (offsets 0x00, 0x0c, 0x18, 0x24) are the four facings. A 0x240-byte 24x24 scratch buffer receives the decoded cel via the cel blitter, is blitted into the caller's window image at offset 0xd21 (column 161, row 10), and the window image is then blitted into the work buffer as a 0x123 x 0xc8 region at offset 0xf.

When the idle animation is armed, the frame counter drives four phases -- frames 0..14 draw the facing-0 sprite at the table's Y positions (the drop and bounce), frames 15..24, 25..34 and 35..44 draw the other three facings standing at row 170 -- after which a separate Y counter starting at 170 walks the sprite upward in steps of 4 until it passes row 10, at which point the frame counter returns to -1 and the sprite goes back to the plain walk cycle.

The work buffer is presented to VGA memory at 0xa0000 (whole 64000-byte copy when the mode flag is clear, otherwise a 0x138 x 0xc0 rect blit at 0xa0504), both remaining buffers are freed, and the last-drawn tick is updated.

Parameters:
  window_image -- pointer to the 320-pitch composed status window image; the unit sprite is stamped into it at offset 0xd21 each frame
  unit_index -- index into the 0x30-byte-per-entry sprite pointer table at 0x60138; overridden by the global at 0x63fb0 when the mode flag at 0x60070 is set
  allow_idle_animation -- non-zero enables the randomly triggered drop/bounce/turn/rise idle sequence

Returns:
  The input code returned by the poller at 0x178f0 once it is <= 0x7f.

=== 00017990 [PLATE]
Modal two-option (left/right) prompt loop.

Algorithm:
1. Allocates a 0x561E byte buffer and copies a 302x73 (0x12E x 0x49) region of
   VGA memory at 0xA9609 into it at stride 302. The caller has already drawn the
   prompt panel there, so this captures the panel bitmap. The four corner pixels
   (offsets 0, 0x12D, 0x54F0, 0x561D) are then cleared to 0 so the later masked
   blit renders the panel with transparent (rounded) corners.
2. Loops until a confirm or cancel key arrives. Each iteration:
   - calls the per-frame update helper at 0002EAB0 and allocates a 0x15180 byte
     (360x240) off-screen work buffer;
   - if the flag at 00060070 is zero, hands the work buffer to 0002BF60 to draw
     the background; otherwise copies the current 312x192 screen area at 0xA0504
     into the work buffer at offset 0x21D8 (x=24, y=24, stride 360);
   - masked-blits the captured panel into the work buffer at offset 0xC4FD
     (x=29, y=140) via 0002F390;
   - if the pointer at 00060120 is non-null, draws it through 000568DB at work
     buffer offset 0x9AD0 (x=32, y=110);
   - draws the two option graphics from the cel sheet at 000643DC at x=249 and
     x=275, y=123. The unselected side uses a fixed cel (13 for the right slot,
     8 for the left); the selected side cycles a 4-frame animation, cels 4..7 on
     the left and 9..12 on the right, indexed by ((frame/3)%4);
   - waits for vertical retrace by polling port 0x3DA (bit 3), blits the work
     buffer back to the screen at 0xA0504 (312x192), spins until the tick
     counter at 00069D64 changes, records it as the animation frame, and frees
     the work buffer;
   - reads a scancode via 000567BE: 0x1C (Enter) or 0x39 (Space) confirms and
     keeps the current selection; 0x01 (Esc) or 0x53 (Del) exits with -1;
     0x4B (Left) selects 0; 0x4D (Right) selects 1. Codes >= 0x7F are ignored.
3. Frees the captured panel buffer and returns the selection.

Selection starts at 0, so the left option is highlighted on entry.

Parameters: none.

Returns: 0 or 1 for the confirmed option (left or right), -1 if cancelled.

=== 000183d0 [PLATE]
Option toggle menu (four boolean game settings).

Algorithm:
Copies 16 zero bytes from 0x00014a9c into a 4-entry menu state array on the stack and clears the selection index. Then loops: for each of the four settings it stores a label id into a 4-entry id array, choosing the ON id when the flag is non-zero and the OFF id otherwise -- 0x0d/0x31 for the CD music flag at 0x00060008, 0x0e/0x32 for the flag at 0x00069d70 (written by fdps_audio_init), 0x0f/0x33 for the flag at 0x00060010, and 0x18/0x3c for the flag at 0x00060158 (also read by fdps_run_village_phase). The OFF id is always the ON id plus 0x24. It then calls the menu widget trio at 0x00016130 (draw), 0x00016200 (run selection, returns the chosen entry or -1) and 0x000161b0 (erase), all with (id array, state array, &selection). A return of -1 ends the loop and the function. Otherwise the flag matching the selection index is XORed with 1; selecting entry 0 additionally calls the audio routine at 0x00030cc0 with (global at 0x00069cf4, 0), and the loop repeats so the labels are redrawn in their new state.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0001892e [EOL]

=== 00018930 [PLATE]
Loads the nine global game data tables into their BSS pointer slots.

Algorithm: performs nine fixed, unconditional calls to the shared file loader at 0x29400. Each call passes the function's own single stack argument through unchanged as the loader's first argument, a literal .dat filename as the second, and the address of a distinct BSS pointer global as the third. The order and the filename/destination pairing are hard-coded: Friaprda.dat -> 0x63fd8, FriLevUp.dat -> 0x63fec, Item.dat -> 0x63fe0, EnemyDat.dat -> 0x63fd4, ProMap.dat -> 0x63fd0, ProEqu.dat -> 0x63fe4, MagicDat.dat -> 0x63ff0, GetMgTab.dat -> 0x63fe8, RankUp.dat -> 0x63fdc. There is no branching, no error handling and no return value inspection; every loader result is discarded.

Parameters: one 32-bit stack argument at [ebp+0x14], never inspected here, forwarded verbatim as the first argument of every loader call.

Returns: nothing. Its effect is entirely through the nine BSS pointer globals in 0x63fd0..0x63ff0, which are zero in the image and therefore filled in at runtime.

=== 00018a19 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0x0] (6-byte NOP) + nop, aligning next function entry to 0x10.

=== 00018a20 [PLATE]
Frees the group of nine global resource buffers held at 0x00063fd0..0x00063ff0.

Algorithm: pushes each of the nine global pointers in a fixed order (0x63fd8, 0x63fec, 0x63fd4, 0x63fd0, 0x63fe0, 0x63fe4, 0x63ff0, 0x63fe8, 0x63fdc) and calls the CRT free helper FUN_0003d478 for each, adjusting the stack by 4 after every call. The pointers are not cleared to NULL afterwards, so the function must be called exactly once. The globals are filled by the matching loader at 0x00018930 (reached from fdps_load_global_resources); each buffer additionally has its own small accessor (0x00018ab0, 0x00018ae0, 0x00018b70, 0x00018bd0, 0x00018c00, ...).

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing.

=== 00018aaf [EOL]

=== 00018b70 [PLATE]
Returns a pointer to one record of the class table loaded from PROMAP.DAT.

Algorithm: multiplies the single stack argument by 10 (the PROMAP.DAT record stride, taken straight from the IMUL immediate), adds it to the class-table base pointer held in the BSS global at 0x63fd0, and returns the sum. There is no bounds check, no null check on the base pointer and no branching; the whole body is one IMUL, one load and one ADD. The base pointer is filled in at runtime by fdps_load_data_tables, which loads ProMap.dat into it, and is released by fdps_free_global_resource_buffers. The table is 410 bytes, i.e. 41 records; record 0 is a default entry, so callers index it as class code + 1.

Parameters: one 32-bit stack argument at [ebp+0x14], the record index (class code + 1).

Returns: pointer to the first byte of the requested 10-byte class record. The result is undefined if the index is out of range or if the table has not been loaded.

=== 0001e3f0 [PLATE]
Advances the battle to the next turn.

Algorithm:
1. Clears the input-enabled flag at 0x00060159, allocates a 0x15180-byte (360x240) offscreen map buffer via 0x0003d375 and renders the battle map into it with 0x0002bf60.
2. Walks every unit (count at 0x00060150, unit record via 0x0002d210). A unit that has kind byte +0x06 == 2, none of flag bits 0x81 set in byte +0x05, zero in bytes +0x25 and +0x26, and current HP (+0x40) below max HP (+0x42) recovers maxhp/5 HP, clamped to max. Each heal draws a floating value over the unit with 0x0001ee10(buffer, unit_index, 0xff00, 3) and records that at least one unit rested.
3. Waits for the VGA vertical retrace by polling port 0x3DA bit 3 (0x0003d4e4), then blits the 312x192 map viewport from buffer+0x21d8 to video memory at 0xa0504 (0x0002f2f0). If any unit rested, plays REST.WAV via 0x0002a1f0. Delays 40 ticks, frees the buffer and repaints the frame (0x0002beb0).
4. Runs the allied-NPC phase: selects side 1 (0x0002e0c0 / 0x0001fa30) and calls 0x00012b20.
5. Shows the EnyPhase.saf banner (0x0001eb00), selects side 0 and runs the enemy phase 0x00012960.
6. Increments the turn counter at 0x00069ce8, shows the PlyPhase.saf banner, clears the cursor-active flag at 0x00069cd0 and selects side 2 (player).
7. Applies per-unit MP regeneration: looks up item 0xa6 then 0xa7 on unit 4, item 0xb1 on unit 8, and item 0xb3 on every unit (0x00034520). When the found slot has bit 0x40 set in unit+0x0a[slot], the unit is not disabled (byte +0x05 == 0) and current MP (+0x44) differs from max MP (+0x46), it gains MP via 0x000275a0(unit, 0x0f), scrolls the view to the unit (0x0002da50), shows a type-0x0d floating value (0x0001f510) and waits for the display to finish (0x0001f340).
8. Re-enables the cursor (0x00069cd0 = 1), centres the view on unit 0 unless 0x000109b0 says otherwise, calls 0x000567b3 and sets the input-enabled flag at 0x00060159.

Every stage after step 3 is guarded by the battle-abort flag at 0x00069da0; when it is non-zero the function returns immediately without re-enabling input.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0001e83c [EOL]

=== 0001eb00 [PLATE]
Play a named full-screen animation entry out of MISC.VFS, wrapped in a fade-out / fade-in of the screen that was showing before.

Algorithm:
1. fdps_vfs_load_entry("MISC.VFS", name) loads the entry and returns its in-memory handle.
2. Allocates two 64000-byte (320x200) buffers via the CRT allocator at 0003d375; both receive the current VGA page at 0xa0000 through the CRT block copy at 0003d514. One buffer keeps the pre-animation screen, the other is scratch for playback.
3. Fade out: for level = 1..5 calls fdps_blit_tint_rect(saved, 320, 0xa0000, 320, 320, 200, DAT_000653f0, DAT_000643f0, 0, level), i.e. redraws the saved image through the tint tables at increasing darkness. Each step first spins on VGA input status port 0x3DA bit 3 (wait for the retrace to start, then to end) and then waits for the tick counter DAT_00069d64 to change, so one fade step lands per frame.
4. Re-snapshots the (now black) screen into the scratch buffer.
5. If the requested name is exactly "PLYPHASE.SAF" (CRT string compare at 00042fe0), calls FUN_0001e840 on the scratch buffer for that entry's extra setup.
6. Calls FUN_0001ecf0(entry, scratch) to actually decode and play the entry, then frees the scratch buffer.
7. Fade in: same loop with level = 5..1 over the buffer holding the pre-animation screen, restoring what was on screen before the call, and frees both remaining allocations.

Parameters:
  name - NUL-terminated entry name inside MISC.VFS, pushed on the stack by the caller.

Returns:
  Nothing. On return the VGA page holds the screen that was showing on entry and every buffer allocated here has been freed.

=== 00022750 [PLATE]
Resets the per-chapter runtime state and brings it back online.

Algorithm:
  1. Clears the chapter-state active flag at 00069cd0 and the shared
     counter at 00069da0.
  2. Calls FUN_00022be0 with the current chapter id held in 00069cf4.
     That helper is called from nowhere else and does the chapter-specific
     re-initialisation.
  3. Zeroes the 0x20-byte state block at 000640d8 (memset-style helper at
     00042cd0, args pushed and popped by the caller).
  4. Zeroes four more counters/indices: 00069ce4, 00069ce0, 00069cd4,
     00069ccc.
  5. Sets the active flag 00069cd0 and the second flag 00069ce8 to 1, then
     calls the shared runtime helper at 000567b3 and returns.

Parameters:
  none. All inputs are read from globals, chiefly the current chapter id at
  00069cf4 which is written by the title screen, the demo, and the save/load
  screens.

Returns:
  void.

Called from the 31 dispatch stubs at 00020e90..00021650 (reached through the
function-pointer table at 00060074, dispatched by fdps_title_screen) and from
fdps_title_demo, so it is the common reset tail every chapter entry runs.

=== 000227d6 [EOL]

=== 000227e0 [PLATE]
Loads the whole field-map resource set for the current chapter.

Algorithm:
1. Releases the resource set left over from the previous chapter: for each of the DAT_00069cdc layers it frees the mpl pointer in DAT_00069cb0[i], the cel pointer in DAT_00069c20[i] and the attr pointer in DAT_00069c98[i].
2. Frees DAT_00060124 and reloads it with "fdetxt%02d.txt" (current chapter DAT_00069cf4 plus one) from Field.vfs -- the chapter text block.
3. Frees DAT_0006013c and reloads it with "map%02d.dat" from Field.vfs. Bytes 1 and 2 of that blob are copied out as the map dimensions into DAT_00064118 and DAT_0006410c.
4. Frees DAT_00060148 and reloads it with "M%02d.dtl" from Field1.vfs.
5. Loads "dsc%02d.dat" from Field2.vfs into a temporary buffer. Its first dword is the layer count, stored into DAT_00069cdc. The remaining data is an array of 32-byte layer descriptors starting at offset 4; for layer i the six dwords at +0x00..+0x14 are scattered into the parallel arrays DAT_00069bf0, DAT_00069c08, DAT_00069c80, DAT_00069c68, DAT_00069c50 and DAT_00069c38, and the bytes at +0x18 and +0x1c into the byte arrays at 0x00069cfe and 0x00069cf8.
6. For each layer i it then loads "m%02d%d.mpl" and "m%02d%d.cel" from Field1.vfs and "attr%02d%d.dat" from Field2.vfs (chapter, i) into DAT_00069cb0[i], DAT_00069c20[i] and DAT_00069c98[i].
7. Frees the descriptor buffer, frees the old working buffer DAT_00060144, reads the 16-bit width and height at offsets 7 and 9 of layer 0's mpl blob, allocates width*height*2+4 bytes for the new working buffer and writes the two dimensions as words at its offsets 0 and 2.
8. Calls FUN_00010b20 to finish initialising the freshly loaded map.

All file names are built with sprintf into a 0x38-byte stack buffer and resolved through fdps_vfs_load_entry, which returns a malloc'd copy of the archive member.

Parameters: none. The chapter index is read from the global DAT_00069cf4.

Returns: nothing. All results are published through the globals listed above.

=== 00022f40 [PLATE]
Uploads VGA DAC colour registers for a contiguous palette index range.

Algorithm: For each index i from start to end inclusive, writes i to the DAC write-index port 0x3C8, then reads three consecutive bytes from rgb (red, green, blue). Each byte has the matching bias added, is clamped to the range 0..63 (values above 0x3F become 0x3F, values below 0 become 0), and is written to the DAC data port 0x3C9. The rgb pointer advances by 3 per entry. All port writes go through the Watcom CRT outp helper at 00042cb8 (MOV EDX,[ESP+4]; MOV AL,[ESP+8]; OUT DX,AL). The DAC index is rewritten every iteration rather than relying on the DAC auto-increment. The bias arguments make this the single upload path used for fades and brightness ramps: passing 0,0,0 uploads the palette unchanged.

Parameters: rgb - pointer to packed RGB triples, one per palette entry, the first triple corresponding to index start; start - first palette index to write; end - last palette index to write, inclusive; r_bias, g_bias, b_bias - signed offsets added to the red, green and blue components before clamping.

Returns: nothing.

=== 00023047 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: 6-byte and 3-byte LEA NOP forms filling to the 16-byte boundary at 00023050.

=== 00023050 [PLATE]
Loads one 12-sprite group from an open .CEL sheet into the global sprite cache.

Algorithm:
1. fseek(fp, 0x0F, SEEK_SET) and fread a fixed 0x2970 bytes (2652 u32 = 221 groups x 12) of the CEL offset table into a temporary malloc'd buffer, then free it after use. The offset table position 0x0F is hardcoded; the header field at 0x05 is never read.
2. Copy the 13 offsets [group_index*12 .. group_index*12+12] into a local array. Entry 12 is the start of the next group and acts as the end sentinel, so group_bytes = off[12] - off[0].
3. If the cache is empty (count at 0x69CF0 == 0): record the group key in the key table at 0x64060, malloc 0x5A0 + group_bytes for the cache buffer at 0x60138, seek to off[0], read group_bytes directly after the 0x5A0 header area, and fill the 12 pointer-table slots with byte offsets 0x5A0 + (off[i] - off[0]). The write cursor at 0x6411C becomes 0x5A0 + group_bytes. Returns slot 0.
4. Otherwise scan the key table for group_index; on a hit return that slot immediately without touching the cache or the count.
5. On a miss, append: store the key, realloc the cache to cursor + group_bytes, seek and read the group at the cursor, fill pointer-table slots (count*12 + i) with cursor + (off[i] - off[0]), advance the cursor and increment the count. Returns the new slot index.

The 0x5A0-byte header region holds exactly 30 slots of 12 u32 offsets; nothing bounds-checks the slot count against 30, so a 31st distinct group would write its pointer table over the cached pixel data. The fixed 0x2970-byte table read is larger than the offset table of a 1920-sprite sheet and spills into pixel data, but only the entries of the requested group are used.

Parameters:
  group_index - index of the 12-sprite group within the sheet (sprite index = group_index * 12 + i)
  fp          - FILE * for the already-opened .CEL sheet; the file position is left inside the sheet

Returns:
  int - cache slot index for the group, valid for the pointer table at the head of the cache buffer

=== 00023bc0 [PLATE]
Adds a character to the unit roster.

Algorithm:
Computes the destination slot as g_roster_base[g_roster_count] where the entry
stride is 0x50 bytes. Looks up two per-character tables by id: a base record
(FUN_00018ab0) and a growth table (FUN_00018ae0). Level is base_record[2].
Max HP = *(int16*)(base+3) + growth[6] * (level - 1);
max MP = *(int16*)(base+5) + growth[8] * (level - 1).
The entry is then filled: +5 = 0, +6 = 2 (side/team tag), +7 and +8 = the
character id, +9 = 0, +0x0a = 0x40, +0x0b = base[0x0c], +0x0c = 0x40,
+0x0d = base[0x0d]. Four two-byte slots at +0x0e..+0x15 are copied from
base[0x0e..0x11]; each slot's flag byte is 0x80 when the source value is 0xff
(empty) and 0 otherwise. Two further slots at +0x16 and +0x18 are set to 0x80
(empty). Four bytes are copied from base+8 to entry+0x1a, +0x1e = 0,
+0x1f = base[0], +0x20 = base[1], +0x21 = level, six bytes at +0x22 are
cleared, +0x31 = 0xff. Three level-scaled stats follow:
+0x37 = *(int16*)(base+0x12) + growth[0] * level,
+0x39 = *(int16*)(base+0x14) + growth[2] * level,
+0x3e = *(int16*)(base+0x16) + growth[4] * level;
+0x3b = base[7], +0x3c = 0. Current and maximum HP are stored at +0x40/+0x42
and current and maximum MP at +0x44/+0x46. Finally FUN_00023ac0 is called with
the new slot index and the roster count is incremented.

Parameters:
  char_id - character/unit identifier, used as the lookup key for both the base
            record and the growth table, and stored in the entry at +7 and +8.

Returns:
  Nothing. The new entry is written into the global roster array and the global
  roster count is incremented.

=== 00023e1f [EOL]
Single 0x90 NOP aligning fdps_load_savegame to an even address.

=== 00023e20 [PLATE]
Loads the saved game FDE.SAV and restores the whole game state from it.

Algorithm:
1. malloc(0x59cb) for the raw save image; on failure print "Out of Memory on get save buffer!!!" and exit(1).
2. fopen("FDE.SAV", "rb"), fread(buf, 1, 0x59cb, fp), fclose.
3. Descramble the image (0x000568b7) and recompute its checksum (0x00056898); if the result differs from the dword stored at buf+0x59c7 the save is corrupt: blank the screen (0x000205b0), blit a warning message (0x0001ff60 with source 0xaa44a at 320,208 size 0x140x0xd0) and run 0x00020820.
4. Fan the image out into the global state:
   - buf+0x08a3, 0xa00 bytes -> buffer pointed to by [0x00064108]
   - reload the palette resource "Fde.pal" from "MISC.VFS" (0x0002a140) into [0x000643bc], freeing the previous one
   - buf+0x30c5 -> [0x00069cf4] (phase/chapter selector), then 0x000227e0
   - fresh malloc(0x8a3) "fieldadr" buffer at [0x0006013c] filled from buf+0x0000; its bytes 1 and 2 become [0x00064118] and [0x0006410c]
   - unit count = buf+0x30c4 -> [0x00060150]; fresh malloc(0x1e00) "ManDataSeg" buffer at [0x00069cd8] filled from buf+0x12a3 with count*0x50 bytes (0x50-byte records)
   - buf+0x30a3, 0x20 bytes -> 0x000640d8
5. fopen("ICON.CEL", "rb"), and for each unit record look up its icon with 0x00023050(record[7], fp), storing the result in record[2]; fclose.
6. Copy the remaining scalar header fields out of buf+0x30c3 (bytes 0, 3..6 scaled by 0x18, 9, dword at 0xa, bytes 0xe..0x11) into their globals, then free the save image.
7. Run the post-load setup (0x0002e910, 0x0002beb0), load "PlyPhase.saf" (0x0001eb00) and enter the play phase with 0x00030cc0(phase, 0).

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing. Does not return at all when a malloc fails, because the error path calls exit(1).

=== 00024490 [PLATE]
Load-game screen: pick a save slot and install it into the live game state.

Algorithm:
1. Sets DAT_00064104 = 1 (mode/state flag while the load screen is up).
2. Loads the cel resource "Load.cel" (string at 0x00061aec) via FUN_00024830 and blits it full screen through FUN_0002fb80 (320x200 to 0xa0000, 16x16 tile params).
3. Calls the slot-selection UI FUN_00024650, passing &slot_index; it returns 1 when the player confirmed a slot. Releases the cel with FUN_0003d478.

When DAT_00060070 is zero it also copies the dword at slot+0x9ec into DAT_00064110.
6. Blits the caller-supplied cel handle back through FUN_0002fb80 to restore the screen and returns the selection result.

Parameters:
  restore_cel - cel/image handle blitted after the load screen closes, restoring the caller's background. Arrives on the stack at [EBP+0x14].

Returns:

=== 0002464d [EOL]

=== 00024650 [PLATE]
Save/load slot selection loop.

Algorithm:
1. Loads the cursor sprite "LoadKon.cel" out of "MISC.VFS" via fdps_vfs_load_entry and keeps the handle for the whole loop.
2. Loops until a result is decided:
   - Reads one keyboard scancode (masked to 8 bits).
   - Scancode 0x48 (Up) or 0x4B (Left): *slot = (*slot + 2) % 3, plays "Beep.wav".
   - Scancode 0x4D (Right) or 0x50 (Down): *slot = (*slot + 1) % 3, plays "Beep.wav".
   - Scancode 0x01 (Esc): result = -1, loop ends.
   - Scancode 0x39 (Space) or 0x1C (Enter): accepted when the global mode flag at 0x00064104 is zero, or when the per-slot entry at 0x000640F8[*slot] is non-zero; on acceptance result = 1 and "morSure.wav" plays. Otherwise the key is ignored.
   - Allocates a 64000-byte off-screen page, copies the caller's background page into it, picks the cursor animation frame as ((tick >> 2) & 3), folding frame 3 back to 1, where tick is the timer counter at 0x00069D64.
   - Blits the cursor with fdps_cel_blit_sprite at x = 10, y = 25 + 52 * (*slot) into the off-screen page, calls FUN_00015be0, waits for vertical retrace by polling bit 3 of port 0x03DA, copies the page to 0xA0000, waits for the timer tick to change, then frees the page.
3. Frees the cursor sprite and returns the result.

Parameters:
  background - pointer to a 64000-byte (320x200) source page that is restored under the cursor every frame.
  slot       - pointer to the currently highlighted slot index; updated in place and left holding the final selection.

Returns:
  1  when the player confirmed the highlighted slot.
  -1 when the player cancelled with Esc.

=== 00024829 [EOL]

=== 00024830 [PLATE]
Builds the save/load menu screen page.

Algorithm:
1. Loads the background cel named by bg_cel_name out of the MISC.VFS archive (fdps_vfs_load_entry) and allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) offscreen page. Blits the cel into the page at pitch 0x140 with all clip/offset arguments zero, then frees the cel.
2. Allocates a 0x59CB-byte save-file buffer and opens FDE.SAV with mode "rb". If the file exists it is read whole (fread size 1, count 0x59CB), post-processed by FUN_000568b7 over the same 0x59CB bytes, and closed; if it does not exist the buffer is filled with 0xFF so every slot reads as empty.
3. For each of the three save slots i (record base = buf + 0x312B + i * 0xA28): the byte at record + 0xA00 acts as the occupied marker. 0xFF stores 0 into the slot-occupied flag array at 0x640F8[i], anything else stores 1. The slot record is then rendered by FUN_00024A40 into the page at row 0x1A + i * 0x34, column 0x0D, pitch 0x140. The save buffer is freed afterwards.
4. If the icon-loaded flag at 0x69CF0 is set, the previously loaded icon block at 0x60138 is freed and the flag cleared. ICON.CEL is then opened "rb" and, for each of the 0x64114 party records (array at 0x64108, stride 0x50), the icon id at record + 7 is passed to FUN_00023050 together with the open file; the file is closed at the end.
5. Returns the offscreen page pointer.

Parameters:
  bg_cel_name - name of the background cel entry inside MISC.VFS; this is what distinguishes the save screen from the load screen, both of which call this function.

Returns:
  Pointer to the newly allocated 64000-byte offscreen page holding the composed screen. Ownership passes to the caller, which is responsible for freeing it.

=== 00027840 [PLATE]
Expands a unit's 40-bit spell bitmask into a list of spell ids.

Algorithm: Looks up the unit record for the incoming id via fdps_get_unit_record (0x0002d210). Walks the five bytes at record offset 0x1a..0x1e; for each byte it tests bits 0..7 from least significant upwards. For every set bit it forms the id byte_index * 8 + bit_index (0..39) and, when the output pointer is non-NULL, stores that byte at the next free slot of the output buffer; the running slot counter is incremented for every set bit whether or not the buffer is written. Nothing bounds-checks the buffer, so it must have room for all 40 ids. Passing NULL as the buffer turns the function into a pure population count of the mask.

Parameters:
  unit_id - index of the unit record, passed unchanged to fdps_get_unit_record.
  out_ids - byte buffer receiving the ids of the set bits in ascending order, or NULL to only count them.

Returns: The number of set bits in the 40-bit mask, i.e. the number of ids written when out_ids is non-NULL.

=== 000282b0 [PLATE]
Algorithm: Builds an 8-entry power-of-two mask table on the stack by copying 8 bytes from the const image at 0x27660 (01 02 04 08 10 20 40 80) with two MOVSD. Calls the record resolver at 0x2d210 with the first argument and keeps the returned base pointer. Splits the second argument into a signed byte index (arg2 / 8, compiled as the SAR/SBB divide-by-power-of-two idiom) and a bit index (arg2 % 8, computed with IDIV by 8 and taking the remainder). ORs the mask for the bit index into the byte at base + 0x1a + byte index, i.e. base[0x1a + n/8] |= 1 << (n % 8). Nothing is read back and nothing is returned. Parameters: arg1 at [EBP+0x14] is the handle or record selector passed straight through to the resolver at 0x2d210; arg2 at [EBP+0x18] is the signed bit number within the flag array at offset 0x1a. Returns: nothing; EAX is left holding the resolved record pointer as a side effect only.

=== 00029220 [PLATE]
C entry point of FDPS. Called by the Watcom startup routine at 0004df4c, which pushes _argc (0006a3b4) and _argv (0006a3b8) and passes this function's return value to exit().

Algorithm:
  1. access("DISK.NO", 0) via INT 21h AH=43h. A non-zero result means the game was
     never installed: print "\nCan't found file 'Disk.No' !!!\a\n" and
     "Please use install function.\n", then exit(1).
  2. fopen("Disk.no", "rt"), three fscanf(fp, "%s", ...) reads and fclose(). The first
     two tokens go to a stack buffer and are discarded; the third is stored in the
     global string buffer at 000643e8. Sets the global at 00069d54 to -1.
  3. Calls the CD-ROM presence check at 0003c636 (returns a 16-bit value, sign-extended
     by CWDE). Anything other than 1 prints
     "Fatal error: CDROM is not install!!!\a\n" and "Check your CDROM please!!!\n",
     then exit(1).
  4. Subsystem init: 000304e0(0x19) and 00029660(), then int386(0x10, &regs, &regs)
     with regs.ax = 0x13 to enter VGA 320x200x256 mode.
  5. Clears the state globals (00069cf0, 00069cdc, 00069da0) and the quit flag at
     000643eb, calls 0002a2b0() once, then loops while the quit flag is zero:
       - 0002bae0() pumps one iteration;
       - the request code left in 00069da0 is dispatched: 0 does nothing, 1 runs
         0002a960() followed by 0002a2b0(), 2 calls the handler taken from the
         function-pointer table at 00060304 indexed by the mode selector in 00069cf4
         and then 00031210();
       - the request code is reset to 0 at the end of every iteration.
  6. Shutdown: 00029440(), 000305a0(), 0003c4a7() (pairs with the CD check),
     int386(0x10, &regs, &regs) with regs.ax = 3 to return to text mode, and finally
     prints "\nThank you for playing Flame Dragon Plus!! \n\n".

Parameters:
  argc, argv - passed on the stack by the startup code but never read by this function.

Returns:
  Nothing meaningful. Control falls off the end of the function, so EAX holds whatever
  the final print call left there; the startup code feeds it to exit(). In C source
  terms this is a main() with no return statement.

=== 00029400 [PLATE]
Fatal-on-failure wrapper around fdps_vfs_load_file.

Algorithm: Calls fdps_vfs_load_file(name, dest) with the file name and destination buffer taken from its stack arguments and writes the returned pointer through the out parameter. If the returned pointer is NULL the routine calls the shutdown/cleanup helper at 000567a0 and then crt_exit(1), so control never comes back on a failed load. On success it simply returns; the loaded pointer is only visible through *out.

Parameters: dest - destination buffer handed to the VFS loader (may be the caller's preallocated storage); name - NUL-terminated file name looked up by the VFS; out - address of the caller's pointer variable that receives the load result.

Returns: nothing. On failure the function does not return, it exits the process with status 1.

=== 00029660 [PLATE]
Loads all globally shared game resources at startup.

Algorithm:
1. Initializes the byte/dword configuration block at 0x6403c-0x6404f (0x10, 0x10, 1, 1, 0x20, 0, 0x10, 0x12), calls the subsystem init at 0x567f0 and allocates a 0xa00 byte scratch buffer into the global at 0x64108.
2. Opens the archive "Misc.vfs" (0x60128). On failure it prints "file not found : Misc.vfs" and exits with status 1.
3. For each of the following entries it looks the name up in the archive and stores the resulting pointer in a global; a NULL result calls the error reporter at 0x567a0 and exits with status 1:
   Fde.pal -> 0x643bc, Fight.pal -> 0x643e4, Cusor.cel -> 0x643b8, Command.cel -> 0x643ac, Shadow.cel -> 0x643dc, IconSts.cel -> 0x643d4, Message.cel -> 0x643d0, Number.cel -> 0x643d8, SelBar.cel -> 0x643c0, LevUp.cel -> 0x643b0, BaseWav.vfs -> 0x643a0, BaseAni.vfs -> 0x643a8, ADWin.cel -> 0x643e0.

5. Loads Bar.cel the same way into a 0xaf8 byte buffer at 0x643c8, copying three sub-images of 0x75 x 8 with stride 0x3a8, then frees the temporary and closes Misc.vfs.
6. Opens the archive "Field.vfs" ("file not found : field.vfs" plus exit 1 on failure), takes Fdetxt.fon -> 0x643cc and Fdetxt00.txt -> 0x643c4, then closes it.
7. Tests for the cached file "FMer1.tmp". If the cache is absent it reads Mer1.tmp (0x4800 bytes into 0x653f0) and Mer2.tmp (0x1000 bytes into 0x643f0) in mode "rb". If the test reports otherwise it builds the tables from Fight.pal and Fde.pal via the routine at 0x2af60 and writes FMer1.tmp/FMer2.tmp and Mer1.tmp/Mer2.tmp in mode "wb" from the same two buffers.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing. Does not return on any resource failure; it exits the process with status 1.

=== 0002a140 [PLATE]
Loads one named entry from a VFS archive into newly allocated memory.

Algorithm:
1. Calls the VFS directory loader at 0x00039ab0 with the archive path (first stack argument). The returned pointer is the in-memory directory table whose entries are 0x1a bytes wide (size at +0x1e, file offset at +0x27, as used by 0x00039bd0).
2. If the directory pointer is NULL, prints "file not found: '%s'" (format string at 0x00061df8) with the archive path and terminates via exit(1).
3. Copies the directory pointer, the entry name and the address of a local result slot into a small stack block and re-loads them - the shape of an inlined helper - then calls the VFS entry reader at 0x00039bd0 with (entry name, directory table). That callee locates the entry by name, opens the backing VFS file, allocates a block of the recorded size, seeks to the recorded offset and reads the entry in. Its return value (the allocated block, or NULL) is stored into the local result slot.
4. If the result is NULL, calls 0x000567a0 and terminates via exit(1).
5. Frees / closes the directory table via 0x0003d478 and returns the allocated block.

Parameters:
  vfs_path   - path of the VFS archive file whose directory is loaded; also the value printed in the "file not found" message.
  entry_name - name of the entry inside that archive to extract.

Returns:
  Pointer to the newly allocated buffer holding the entry's bytes. The function never returns on failure; it exits the process instead. The caller owns the returned block.

=== 0002a1f0 [PLATE]
Play a sound effect by resource name.

Algorithm:
  1. Call the sound pack directory lookup at 00039cf0 with the pack base held
     in the global at 000643a0, the caller supplied name, and the address of a
     local dword that receives the entry size. The lookup walks fixed 26 byte
     directory entries, compares the entry name against the caller name and
     returns pack_base + entry_data_offset, or 0 when the name is absent.
  2. If the lookup returned a non-null pointer, call the sample playback
     helper at 00030a00 with (sample, 1, -1, -1). That helper scans the eight
     AIL sample voices for one whose status is not the playing status, then
     initialises and starts the sample on it. The two -1 arguments select the
     default volume and pan path.
  3. A missing name is silently ignored; no sound is played and nothing is
     reported.

Parameters:
  name - NUL terminated resource name of the sample inside the loaded sound
         pack.

Returns:
  Nothing. EAX on exit is leftover from the last call and is not a result.

=== 0002a2b0 [PLATE]
Title screen and opening attract loop; the only caller is main.

Algorithm:
1. Reset the input/keyboard state, set the global 'in title screen' flag at 0x60159 and clear 0x64110.
2. Try to open the save file "FDE.SAV" ("rb") with the stdio wrappers, read 0x59cb bytes into a malloc'd buffer, close it, then compute a checksum over the buffer and compare it against the dword stored at buffer+0x59c7. If the checksum matches and the byte at buffer+0x30c5 is not 0xff, clear menu-state entries 1 and 2 of the local 4-byte menu flag array (initialised from the dword at 0x29210, where 0 means selectable and 2 means greyed out), i.e. enable the save-dependent menu entries.
3. Outer attract loop, repeated until a menu entry has been chosen: load "Dynasty.pal" from MISC.VFS, allocate a 0x16480 byte animation buffer and load "Logo.saf"; play the animation frame by frame, blitting the frame at offset 0x2298 to 0xa0000 (320x200, source pitch 0x170) after waiting on the VGA vertical retrace bit of port 0x3da and on the timer tick counter at 0x69d64. Any key with scancode < 0x80 aborts the animation.
4. Format "FD%d" with the alternating track index and start that music track, then enter the title menu loop: load "StSel.cel", "StBoard.cel" and "StBoard.pal" from MISC.VFS, allocate a 64000 byte offscreen buffer, and run a menu frame loop with a 0x6d6 tick idle counter. Up (0x48) and Down (0x50) move the selection modulo 4; Enter (0x1c) or Space (0x39) accepts, but only when the selected entry's flag byte is 0. Each frame clears the offscreen buffer, draws the board cel, draws the four selection cels using frame index i*3 + (i == selection ? 1 : flags[i]) at y = 0x48 + i*0x11, waits for retrace and copies the buffer to 0xa0000.
5. On exit from the menu loop, fade the palette to black over 10 retrace-synchronised steps, clear video memory, free all buffers and reset the palette. If the idle counter did not reach zero, dispatch on the selection: 0 starts a new game through the state-machine function pointer table at 0x60074 (index 0x69cf4), 1 opens the load-game screen and, on success, runs the post-load entry, 2 runs the other save-dependent entry, and 3 sets the quit flag at 0x643eb. If the idle counter did reach zero the demo/attract routine runs instead and the whole cycle repeats with the other music track.

Parameters: none.

Returns: the index of the title menu entry that was finally chosen (0-3).

=== 0002a956 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] (6) + lea edx,[edx+eiz*1+0] (4), aligns fdps_show_game_over to 0x2a960

=== 0002a960 [PLATE]
Game Over screen sequence.

Algorithm:
1. Allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) buffer and copies the current VGA frame at 0xA0000 into it, so the screen the player was looking at can be redrawn.
2. Loads the resource "GameOver.saf" (0x61e68) out of the archive "MISC.VFS" (0x60128) via the VFS loader at 0002a140, and allocates a 0x16480-byte working surface whose pixel area begins at offset 0x2298.
3. Fills a 0x50-byte on-stack descriptor (pitch 0x170, 0xf8, 0x18, 0x18, plus the loaded resource pointer) and loops: blit the saved frame into the work surface, run the draw call at 00014140, wait for VGA vertical retrace by polling port 0x3DA (bit 3) low then high, blit the work surface to 0xA0000, wait for the timer tick counter at 00069d64 to advance, and poll the input routine at 00014550. The loop ends when input returns non-zero.
4. Runs a 16-step fade: for each step i the fade blit at 00030010 is called with the two 4 KB lookup tables at 000653f0 / 000643f0 and level 0x6f, the descriptor is redrawn twice (once with the table pair, once with mode 0x1f), and each step is again paced by retrace and by the tick counter.
5. Frees the work surface, the loaded resource and the saved frame, then calls 000567b3 and 000567a0, which are the program termination stubs.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing. The trailing RET is present but is not expected to be reached, because the two calls that precede it terminate the program.

Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0x0] (8d 40 00), aligning fdps_title_demo to 0x2ac10.

=== 0002ac10 [PLATE]
Title screen attract-mode demo sequence.

Algorithm:
1. Sets three globals: DAT_00069cf4 = 0x19, DAT_00060159 = 0 (demo-active flag), DAT_00064114 = 0.
2. Calls the loader at 00023bc0 twelve times with ids 0,1,8,9,2,10,3,11,4,12,5,6, then calls 00022750 once and clears DAT_00069cd0.
3. Fetches record 3 via the table accessor at 0002d210 and stores 4 into its byte at offset 0; calls 00036b60(0,0x0b,0) and 000282b0 twice (1,0x27) and (4,0x0b).
4. For slots 0..11 fetches the record via 0002d210 and writes byte[6]=1, word[0x40]=word[0x42]=2000, word[0x44]=word[0x46]=word[0x48]=800, word[0x4c]=400 -- a canned set of demo values so nothing in the demo can be knocked out.
5. Flushes the keyboard ring buffer via the stub at 000567b3 (write index := read index), then runs an outer pass counter initialised to 2. Each pass walks i = 0 .. DAT_00060150-1: record = 0002d210(i); if 000109b0(i) returns 0, calls 00010010(i, record->byte[6]); when i == 0x0c it calls the frame routine 0002db50. After each unit it polls the key stub at 000567be, which returns 0xff when the 10-entry ring at 0x0007000f is empty; any value below 0x7f is a real keypress, which forces the pass counter to 1 and breaks out. Each pass ends with 0002db50 and decrements the counter.
6. On exit calls 0003d478(DAT_00064108), clears DAT_00064114, sets DAT_00060159 = 1, blanks 64000 bytes at 0x000a0000 (mode 13h frame buffer) via the fill routine at 00042cd0, and flushes the key buffer again.

Parameters: none. The stack frame is PUSH EBX/ESI/EDI/EBP, MOV EBP,ESP, SUB ESP,0x0c with three locals and no read above the frame pointer.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0002af54 [EOL]

=== 0002af60 [PLATE]
Builds the global palette helper tables used by the shading and colour-matching code.

Algorithm:
1. Copies the 18-entry brightness coefficient table at 0002ae40 onto the stack. The coefficients are {0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,16,15,14,13,12,11,10,9,8}, indexed by shade level.
2. For each of the 256 palette entries: reads the three bytes (r,g,b) of the source palette (each a 6-bit VGA component, scaled by 4 to 8 bits), packs them as r<<16|g<<8|b, then calls the three component extractors (0002ae90 red, 0002aec0 green, 0002aef0 blue). Each component is masked with 0xF0 and repositioned so the result holds the high nibble of red in bits 16-19, green in bits 8-11 and blue in bits 0-3. Level row 0 of the ramp table (000653f0) is zeroed (black) and this packed value is stored into level row 1 (000657f0, i.e. 000653f0 + 0x400).
3. For levels 2..17, each of the 256 packed row-1 values is multiplied by the level coefficient and stored at 000653f0[level*256 + index]. Because each channel occupies only the low nibble of its own byte, a multiply by up to 16 scales all three channels at once without carrying between them.
4. Fills the 16x16x16 inverse lookup cube at 000643f0: for every (r,g,b) triple of 4-bit components (passed as component*4) it calls the nearest-colour matcher at 0002b1b0 with the source palette and stores the returned palette index as a byte, written sequentially over 4096 entries.

Parameters:
  palette - pointer to 256 packed 3-byte RGB entries (VGA 6-bit components) in r,g,b order.

Returns:
  Nothing meaningful. EAX holds a leftover loop counter; the decompiler models it as an int return but no caller uses it.

=== 0002b1a4 [EOL]
Alignment padding: two 6-byte Watcom NOP forms (lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0]).

=== 0002b1b0 [PLATE]
Finds the palette index whose RGB triple is closest to a requested color.

Algorithm: keeps a running best squared distance seeded with 10000000 and a running best index seeded with 0x100. Walks a fixed 256 iterations over the palette, consuming three consecutive bytes per entry (red, green, blue in that order) through an advancing byte cursor. For each entry it forms dr = r - pal[0], dg = g - pal[1], db = b - pal[2] and computes dr*dr + dg*dg + db*db as signed 32-bit arithmetic. If that sum is strictly less than the current best it becomes the new best and the loop counter becomes the new best index. The loop count is hardcoded to 256 and is not derived from any argument, so the palette buffer must hold at least 768 bytes. The strict less-than test makes the first of several equally close entries win. The 0x100 seed can never survive because the maximum possible distance is 3*255*255 = 195075, well below the 10000000 seed, so the function always returns a value in 0..255.

Parameters: r, g, b are the target color components, passed as full 32-bit ints and only compared as such (the palette bytes are zero-extended, the targets are not masked). palette points at the first byte of a 256-entry, 3-bytes-per-entry RGB table.

Returns: the 0..255 index of the closest palette entry.
