=== 0002b4f0 [PLATE]
Interactive battle-map cursor / target selection loop.

Algorithm:
Reads the map dimensions from the header at 0x00060144 (tile width at +0, tile height at +2) and converts them to pixel extents by multiplying by the 0x18 tile size. When mode == 6 the candidate list length is stashed and then forced to zero, so list cycling is disabled in that mode. If a candidate list is supplied and the first candidate's unit record byte at +7 is not 'y', the cursor is snapped onto that candidate via fdps_map_cursor_move_to using its tile coordinates from unit record bytes +0 and +1 (unit records are 0x50 bytes, base pointer at 0x00069cd8).
FUN_00056799 yields the pointer to the current keyboard scancode byte; it is reset to 0xFF before the loop and after a cancel. Each iteration calls fdps_cd_music_repeat_poll, then reads the scancode:
  0x01 (Esc) / 0x53 (Del)  -> result = -1, loop ends
  0x39 (Space) / 0x1C (Enter) -> confirm. In mode 6 the whole unit array (count at 0x00060150) is scanned for a unit standing on the cursor tile that fails FUN_000109b0; if none blocks, FUN_00018b70 and FUN_0002ba00 are consulted and the tile cost byte read through 0x00069d09 must be below 0x14 for the confirm to succeed (returns 1). Outside mode 6, mode 5 confirms nothing; otherwise FUN_0002ba00 resolves the tile under the cursor and the resolved index at 0x00069d0c must not be 0xFF; mode 4 then returns 1 directly, other modes call FUN_000109f0 with the cursor tile, the clamped value of 0x00069cd0 and the mode, and set the result to 1 when it accepts.
  0x2C (Z) / 0x4C (numpad 5) -> advance to the next entry of the candidate list (wrapping) and snap the cursor to that unit.
  0x48/0x50/0x4B/0x4D (arrows) -> move the cursor one tile (0x18 pixels) in Y (0x00069ccc) or X (0x00069cd4), clamped to the map extents, each move playing "Beep.wav" through FUN_0002a1f0. A repeat counter suppresses movement while the same scancode has been held for 1..5 iterations, giving the usual key-repeat delay.
After key handling the view origin (0x00069ce0 for Y, 0x00069ce4 for X) is scrolled by whole tiles so the cursor stays inside the visible window, and fdps_render_view_frame redraws.

Parameters:
  mode  - selection mode; 4, 5 and 6 take special confirm paths, other values are passed on to FUN_000109f0
  count - number of entries in the candidate list; 0 disables list cycling, and mode 6 forces it to 0 after saving it as the acting unit index
  list  - array of unit indices to cycle through with the cycle key

Returns:
  1 when the player confirmed a valid selection, -1 when the player cancelled. Selected tile coordinates are left in the cursor globals 0x00069cd4 (X) and 0x00069ccc (Y).

=== 0002b9f5 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov ebx,ebx filling 11 bytes to the 0002ba00 boundary.

=== 0002ba00 [PLATE]
Loads the per-cell map information for one battle map tile into the shared "current tile" globals.

Algorithm:
1. Reads the terrain layer pointer at 0x00069cb0. The layer header stores the map width as a 16-bit value at offset 7 and the cell array (one 16-bit tile id per cell) starts at offset 0xb. The linear cell index is y * width + x; the tile id read from that slot is stored to the global at 0x00069d04.
2. Uses that tile id to index the tile attribute table pointed to by 0x00069c98: entries are 4 bytes each and begin at offset 0x11 of the table. The four bytes of the entry are copied out individually, in the order byte2 -> 0x00069d09, byte0 -> 0x00069d08, byte1 -> 0x00069d0a, byte3 -> 0x00069d0b.
3. Indexes a second layer pointed to by 0x00060144 with the same linear cell index (2 bytes per cell, array at offset 4) and copies the high byte of that cell to the global at 0x00069d0c.
4. Indexes a third layer pointed to by 0x00060148, whose own width field lives at offset 7 of its header and whose cell array is one byte per cell starting at offset 0x10. The byte is zero-extended and stored to the 16-bit global at 0x00069d06.
The function performs no bounds checking on x or y and calls nothing.

Parameters:
  x - map column index, pushed last by the caller.
  y - map row index.

Returns:
  Nothing. All results are delivered through the globals at 0x00069d04, 0x00069d06, 0x00069d08, 0x00069d09, 0x00069d0a, 0x00069d0b and 0x00069d0c.

=== 0002badb [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] + mov ecx,ecx, aligning next function entry to 16 bytes.

=== 0002bae0 [PLATE]
Player-phase interactive loop on the battle map.

Algorithm:
Obtains the shared keyboard-state byte via the low-level key accessor at 0x56799 and primes it to 0xFF. Reads the map dimensions from the header pointed to by 0x60144 (word 0 = width, word 1 = height) and converts both to pixel extents by multiplying by the 24-pixel tile size. Then loops until the local exit flag becomes non-zero.

Each iteration polls input (0x30c50), reads the current scancode and maintains an auto-repeat counter: the same scancode as last time increments the counter, a different one resets it and records the new code. An action is only taken when the counter is 0 (first press) or greater than 5 (auto-repeat has kicked in); F1 (0x3B) and F2/Home (0x3C/0x47) are handled on the suppressed path instead.

Scancode dispatch:
  0x48/0x50 (up/down)    move the cursor Y at 0x69CCC by one tile, clamped to the map height
  0x4B/0x4D (left/right) move the cursor X at 0x69CD4 by one tile, clamped to the map width
                         each accepted move calls the feedback routine at 0x2A1F0 with the descriptor at 0x61E78
  0x01/0x2C/0x4C/0x53    cycle forward through the unit table: starting from the saved rotation index,
                         scan up to unit-count (0x60150) entries of the 0x50-byte records based at 0x69CD8
                         and stop at the first with (flags[+5] & 0x85) == 0 and side[+6] == 2, then centre
                         on it via 0x2D7C0 with its tile coordinates scaled by 24, saving the next index
  0x1C/0x39 (Enter/Space) clear the key latch (0x567B3), resolve the unit under the cursor (0x2DAA0);
                         with no unit, open the battle menu (0x14AB0) and take its return as the exit flag;
                         with a unit that is friendly (side[+6] == 2), not marked done (flags[+5] & 0x80)
                         and with byte +0x26 clear, open the unit command menu (0x15470) and, if the
                         phase-end flag 0x69DA0 is still clear, run the post-command step (0x2EA10);
                         otherwise show the read-only unit info screen (0x16AA0)
  0x3B (F1)              invoke the routine at 0x2E160
  0x3C/0x47 (F2/Home)    resolve the unit under the cursor and show its info screen (0x16AA0)

After dispatch the camera origin (X at 0x69CE4, Y at 0x69CE0) is scrolled by one tile whenever the cursor comes within one tile of a viewport edge, clamped so the viewport (0x138 by 0xC0 pixels) stays inside the map. The frame is then drawn by 0x2BEB0, and the loop exits once the phase-end flag at 0x69DA0 is non-zero.

Parameters:
None. The decompiler's param_1/param_2/unaff_EBX are artifacts; no incoming stack slot or register is read before being written.

Returns:
Nothing.

=== 0002bead [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0x0] (8d 40 00) aligning next function entry to 16 bytes.

=== 0002beb0 [PLATE]
Renders and presents one frame of the framed main view.

Algorithm:
1. malloc(0x15180 = 86400) a scratch scene buffer. The buffer is a composite: the first 0x21d8 (8664) bytes are header/state, the pixel plane starts at +0x21d8 and is 360 bytes per scan line.
2. FUN_0002bf60(buf) fills/initialises the buffer (shared helper, 21 callers).
3. FUN_0002dcf0(buf) composes this view's content into it (called from nowhere else).
4. Busy-wait on inp(0x3DA) until bit 3 is set, i.e. until vertical retrace begins.
5. FUN_0002eab0() runs inside the retrace window (4 callers; typical of DAC/palette programming).
6. Busy-wait on inp(0x3DA) until bit 3 clears, i.e. until retrace ends.
7. FUN_0002f2f0(buf + 0x21d8, 0x168, 0xA0504, 0x140, 0x138, 0xC0) blits the composed image to VGA memory: source stride 360, destination 0xA0000 + 0x504 (row 4, column 4 of a 320-wide mode 13h screen), destination stride 320, 312 x 192 pixels. That is exactly the interior of a 4-pixel border around the 320x200 screen.
8. Spin until the interrupt-driven tick counter DAT_00069d64 differs from the last-seen value DAT_00069d10, then latch DAT_00069d10 = DAT_00069d64. This paces the caller to one tick per frame.
9. free(buf) and return.

Parameters: none. Everything drawn is taken from global state, which is why 41 different call sites can invoke it with no arguments.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0002bf5f [EOL]
single-byte 0x90 alignment pad between fdps_render_view_frame and fdps_draw_scene_layers

=== 0002bf60 [PLATE]
Composites the scrolling layer table into a scene buffer.

The layer table is a fixed 6-slot structure-of-arrays block in .data, each array 6 dwords or 6 bytes long and packed back to back: pos_x[6] at 0x69bf0, pos_y[6] at 0x69c08, unknown_a[6] at 0x69c20, vel_y[6] at 0x69c38, vel_x[6] at 0x69c50, scroll_factor_y[6] at 0x69c68, scroll_factor_x[6] at 0x69c80, unknown_b[6] at 0x69c98, image[6] at 0x69cb0, mode[6] (bytes) at 0x69cf8 and depth[6] (bytes) at 0x69cfe. The number of active slots is DAT_00069cdc.

Algorithm:
1. Tick gate: if the interrupt tick counter DAT_00069d64 differs from the last value this function
   latched in DAT_00069d14, add vel_x[i] to pos_x[i] and vel_y[i] to pos_y[i] for every active slot,
   then latch DAT_00069d14 = DAT_00069d64. Position therefore advances once per tick regardless of
   how many times per tick the function is called.
2. FUN_0002c220 fills a local 6-entry array with the slot indices in draw order.
3. Pass one, in that order: for each slot whose depth byte is below 10, compute
   x = (DAT_00069ce4 * scroll_factor_x[i] + pos_x[i]) >> 3 and
   y = (DAT_00069ce0 * scroll_factor_y[i] + pos_y[i]) >> 3 -- a per-slot multiple of the two global
   camera/scroll values plus the slot's own accumulated offset, in 1/8 pixel fixed point -- and call
   FUN_0002c330(buf, image[i], unknown_a[i], DAT_00060144, x, y, unknown_b[i], mode[i]).
4. Draw the mid-layer content into the same buffer: FUN_0002c6a0(buf) then FUN_0002d240(buf, 0).
5. Pass two: the identical loop and blit call for every slot whose depth byte is above 10, so those
   slots land on top of the mid-layer content. Depth exactly 10 is drawn by neither pass.

Parameters:
  buf -- destination scene buffer, passed straight through to every drawing callee.

Returns: nothing.

Alignment padding: three Watcom multi-byte NOP forms (lea reg,[reg+0]) filling 15 bytes to the 16-byte entry of FUN_0002c220.

=== 0002d204 [EOL]

=== 0002d210 [PLATE]
Accessor for the global unit-record array.

Algorithm: Multiplies the index argument by the record stride 0x50 (80 bytes) and adds it to the base pointer held in the global at 0x00069cd8, returning the address of that record. No bounds check is performed and the base pointer is not tested for NULL. The array is allocated and filled by fdps_load_savegame, read throughout battle and menu code, and released by fdps_shutdown_free_resources.

Parameters: index - zero-based record index; the caller is responsible for keeping it in range.

Returns: Pointer to the start of the indexed 0x50-byte record. Undefined behaviour if the global base pointer has not been initialised.

=== 0002d235 [EOL]

=== 0002d2c9 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte NOP form 8d 80 00 00 00 00 plus 90, aligning next function entry to 0x10.

=== 0002d2d0 [PLATE]
Plays back a movement path one step at a time.

Algorithm:
Iterates a local counter i from 0 to count-1. For each i it loads the
byte at steps[i] and zero-extends it into a local step code, then
dispatches on that code: 0 calls FUN_0002d360, 1 calls FUN_0002d590,
2 calls FUN_0002d480, and any other value falls through to the default
handler FUN_0002d6a0. Every handler receives the same single argument,
the ctx pointer passed in as the first stack argument; the step byte
itself is not forwarded. All four handlers share the same callee set
(a shared helper pair plus fdps_render_view_frame), so each step code
selects a variant of the same per-step animation-and-redraw work.
The loop body does nothing else; the function has no early exit and no
return value.

Parameters:
ctx    - first stack argument, the animation/actor context handed
         unchanged to every step handler.
steps  - second stack argument, pointer to a byte array of step codes,
         read one byte per iteration.
count  - third stack argument, number of step codes to play back;
         a value <= 0 makes the function a no-op.

Returns:
Nothing. Arguments arrive on the stack and the caller removes them.

=== 0002d353 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / nop, 13 bytes to 16-byte boundary

=== 0002d47e [EOL]
Alignment filler: 8b c0 (mov eax,eax), 2-byte nop padding to align next function entry.

=== 0002d587 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] (6-byte nop) + lea edx,[edx+0] (3-byte nop), aligns FUN_0002d590 to 16 bytes

=== 0002d695 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov ebx,ebx, aligns FUN_0002d6a0 to 0x20

=== 0002d7bf [EOL]
Single 0x90 NOP aligning the next function entry to a 16-byte boundary.

=== 0002d7c0 [PLATE]
Animated move of the map cursor to a target pixel position, with viewport follow.

Algorithm:
Reads the map dimensions from the header pointed to by DAT_00060144 (word[0] = width in tiles, word[1] = height in tiles) and converts them to pixels by multiplying by the 24-pixel tile size. Computes dx = target_x - cursor_x (DAT_00069cd4) and dy = target_y - cursor_y (DAT_00069ccc); returns immediately when both are zero. Picks the axis with the larger absolute delta as the major axis and derives the step count n = abs(major delta) / 24, i.e. one iteration per tile of travel. The major axis advances by (major delta / n) each step; the minor axis uses a running accumulator that adds (acc / n) and carries (acc % n) forward, so the two axes stay proportional without floating point.

After each step the viewport origin is clamped so the cursor stays inside its scroll margins: vertically the cursor must sit between 0x18 and 0x90 pixels below the view top (DAT_00069ce0), horizontally between 0x18 and 0xd8 pixels right of the view left (DAT_00069ce4). When a clamp pushes the view past the map edge it is pulled back to map_pixel_height - 0xc0 or map_pixel_width - 0x138 (the 192x312 visible map window), and negative origins are pinned to 0. A local flag records whether the viewport actually moved; the map is redrawn through FUN_0002beb0 when it moved or when the forced-redraw flag DAT_00069cd0 is non-zero.

Parameters:
  target_x - destination cursor X in pixels (map space, 24 pixels per tile)
  target_y - destination cursor Y in pixels (map space, 24 pixels per tile)

Returns:
  void. Side effects are on the globals: cursor position DAT_00069cd4 / DAT_00069ccc and viewport origin DAT_00069ce4 / DAT_00069ce0.

=== 0002da4f [EOL]
Single 0x90 NOP aligning FUN_0002da50 to an even address.

=== 0002da97 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] (6-byte nop) + lea edx,[edx+0] (3-byte nop), aligning next function to 0x10.

=== 0002daa0 [PLATE]
Find the index of the battle unit standing on the cursor tile.

Algorithm:
Iterates i from 0 to the unit count at DAT_00060150, walking the unit array whose base pointer is held in DAT_00069cd8 with a stride of 0x50 bytes per record. For each record it loads the unsigned byte at offset 0 (tile column) and the unsigned byte at offset 1 (tile row) and compares them with the cursor position converted from pixels to tiles: DAT_00069cd4 / 24 and DAT_00069ccc / 24, where 24 (0x18) is the map tile size in pixels. When both coordinates match, it calls FUN_000109b0(i); a return value of zero means the unit qualifies, and the loop stops and returns i. Any mismatch, or a non-zero predicate result, advances to the next record. Falling off the end of the array returns -1.

Parameters:
None. All inputs come from globals: DAT_00069cd8 (unit array base pointer, allocated elsewhere and released by fdps_shutdown_free_resources), DAT_00060150 (unit count), DAT_00069cd4 and DAT_00069ccc (cursor pixel coordinates, moved read-modify-write by fdps_battle_player_phase_loop).

Returns:
The zero-based index of the matching unit, or -1 when no unit on the cursor tile qualifies.

=== 0002db4f [EOL]
Single 0x90 NOP aligning fdps_units_clear_status_bit7 entry to a 16-byte boundary.

=== 0002db50 [PLATE]
Clears status bit 7 (0x80) on every battle unit.

Algorithm: iterates i from 0 while i < the global unit count at 0x00060150. For each i it forms the unit record pointer as (*(char **)0x00069cd8) + i * 0x50, the same base pointer and 0x50 stride used by the battle unit code, and applies AND 0x7f to the byte at unit offset +5, clearing bit 7 while leaving the other status bits intact. No bounds check beyond the count, no early exit, and no other field is touched.

Parameters: none. Both the unit array base pointer and the unit count are read from globals.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0002db97 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] filling to the 16-byte entry of fdps_cel_blit_sprite.

=== 0002dba0 [PLATE]
Draw one sprite from a .CEL sheet into a linear 8-bit surface.

Algorithm:
  Reads the sheet-wide sprite size from the .CEL header: signed 16-bit width
  at cel+0x07 and signed 16-bit height at cel+0x09, sign-extended to int.
  Resolves the pixel stream as cel + *(int *)(cel + index * 4 + 0x0F); the
  offset-table position 0x0F is hardcoded, so the header field at 0x05 that
  stores the same value is never read, and neither are the magic, the version,
  the sprite count at 0x0B nor the encoding tag at 0x0D -- there is no format
  validation at all here.
  Computes the destination pointer as dest_base + y * pitch + x, then calls the
  RLE blitter at 0x568db with (stream, dest, width, height, pitch, mode, arg8).
  The blitter is a 13-way dispatch over mode whose variants differ only in
  palette remapping and flipping; the pixel stream format is the 4-op RLE
  described in resource_info/cel.md.
  All arguments arrive on the stack and are read through the EBP frame; the
  callee's seven arguments are cleaned by this function (ADD ESP,0x1C) and this
  function's own arguments are cleaned by its callers, so both sides are cdecl.
  EBX, ESI and EDI are saved and restored but never used.

Parameters:
  cel       pointer to the whole .CEL image loaded in memory (header at offset 0)
  index     zero-based sprite index into the sheet's offset table
  dest_base base of the destination 8-bit surface
  pitch     bytes per destination row
  x         destination column of the sprite's left edge
  y         destination row of the sprite's top edge
  mode      selector forwarded to the blitter's 13-way dispatch (palette remap
            and flip variants)
  arg8      second value forwarded unchanged to the blitter; meaning not settled

Returns:
  Nothing. Draws directly into the caller's surface.

=== 0002dc15 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov ebx,ebx before 0002dc20

=== 0002dce8 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] (6-byte nop) + mov edx,edx (2-byte nop), aligns next entry to 0x10.

=== 0002dcf0 [PLATE]
Draws the battle-map cursor information panel into the supplied frame buffer.

Algorithm:
1. Computes the cursor position in tile units relative to the scroll origin: col = (DAT_00069cd4 - DAT_00069ce4) / 24, row = (DAT_00069ccc - DAT_00069ce0) / 24 (24 = tile size in pixels).
2. Returns immediately unless both panel-enable flags DAT_00060158 and DAT_00060159 are non-zero.
3. Calls FUN_0002ba00 with the absolute cursor tile (DAT_00069cd4 / 24, DAT_00069ccc / 24) to refresh the data the panel displays.
4. Chooses the panel X origin DAT_0006016c so the panel dodges the cursor: row > 4 and col < 2 selects x = 0x124, row > 4 and col > 10 selects x = 0x19; otherwise the previous value is kept.
5. Blits the panel background cel DAT_000643e0 (width 0xa0) at x, then cel DAT_00069c20 frame DAT_00069d04 (width 0xaf) at x + 9, both into the destination with pitch 0x168.
6. Emits two text fields via FUN_00017530 at byte offsets 0xe6b3 and 0x11d8b (plus x), using the font/colour tables at 0x60040 and 0x60058 indexed by DAT_00069d09.
7. Calls fdps_battle_find_unit_at_cursor. On -1 nothing further is drawn.
8. Otherwise takes the 0x50-byte unit record at DAT_00069cd8 + index * 0x50, derives the walk animation phase from DAT_00069d64 >> 2 & 3 (phase 3 folded back to 1), forms the sprite slot record[2] * 12 + phase, resolves it through the relative offset table at DAT_00060138, and blits the 24x24 sprite at offset 0xf621 + x via FUN_000568db.
9. Sets the text colour global DAT_0006000c to 3 when current HP (record+0x40) differs from max HP (record+0x42), prints the current HP as a 4-digit field at offset 0x10b39 + x, then restores DAT_0006000c to 0.

Parameters:
  dest - destination frame buffer, pitch 0x168 bytes per row; all draw offsets are byte offsets into it.

Returns:
  Nothing.

=== 0002df85 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov ebx,ebx, 11 bytes to the 16-byte boundary at 0002df90

=== 0002e02d [EOL]

=== 0002e0ba [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte lea eax,[eax+0] NOP between FUN_0002e030 and FUN_0002e0c0

=== 0002e152 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov eax,eax, 14 bytes to 16-byte boundary

=== 0002e160 [PLATE]
Battlefield overview screen, entered from the player phase.

Algorithm:
1. Allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) off-screen frame buffer and a 0x4000-byte array of 64x64 tile-graphic pointers; calls the minimap tile-graphics loader at 0002e640 for the shared tile source.
2. Reads map dimensions from the map header at DAT_00069cb0 (+7 = width, +9 = height) and the camera pixel origin from DAT_00069ce4 / DAT_00069ce0, converting to tile units by dividing by the 24-pixel tile size. Marker scale defaults to 4 px per tile (virtual width 0x280); maps taller than 40 tiles drop to 3 px per tile (virtual width 0x380).
3. Fills the pointer array: for each tile (x,y) calls the tile lookup at 0002ba00, then takes the resulting tile index from DAT_00069d04 and stores tilesrc + 6 + index * 0x240 (24*24 bytes per tile).
4. Plays a 7-step zoom-out animation: each step interpolates source rect and scale between the camera view and the full map, calls the scaled map blitter at 0002e710 into the off-screen buffer and copies 64000 bytes to 0xa0000.
5. Flushes the keyboard (000567b3) then loops on 000567be until it returns a value <= 0x7f (a real key). Each time the frame counter DAT_00069d64 advances, it redraws all DAT_00060150 units from the unit array at DAT_00069cd8 (stride 0x50): units with bit 0 of byte +5 set are skipped; byte +0 / +1 are tile x / y, byte +6 selects a faction colour base from the table {0x28, 0xb4, 0xd6} copied from 0002b294. A marker square is drawn by 0002e5e0 at (0xa0 + x*scale - width*scale/2, 0x64 + y*scale - height*scale/2). The map cursor (DAT_00069cd4 / DAT_00069ccc divided by 24) is drawn last with colour base 0x82. A shared brightness offset ping-pongs between 0 and 7 each frame, making every marker pulse.
6. Plays the 6-step zoom-in animation in reverse and frees the frame buffer, the pointer array and the tile source.

Parameters: none; all state comes from the battle globals listed above.
Returns: nothing.

=== 0002e5d6 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte lea eax,[eax+0] plus 4-byte lea edx,[edx+0] multi-byte NOPs

=== 0002e63f [EOL]
Single-byte 0x90 NOP alignment filler before fdps_cel_expand_sheet_24x24 at 0002e640.

=== 0002e640 [PLATE]
Expand the whole chapter CEL sheet into a newly allocated uncompressed 24x24 shape array.

Algorithm:
  Takes no arguments; the source sheet is the global CEL pointer at 0x00069c20,
  which is loaded by fdps_load_field_chapter_resources and released by
  fdps_shutdown_free_resources.
  Reads the sheet's sprite count as a signed 16-bit value at cel+0x0B (the same
  field fdps_cel_blit_sprite documents but never reads) and sign-extends it to
  int, then allocates count * 0x240 + 6 bytes through the CRT allocator at
  0x0003d375. 0x240 is 576 = 24 * 24, one byte per pixel, so the sheet's sprites
  are assumed to be 24x24; nothing here validates that against the width/height
  fields in the source header.
  On allocation failure it prints "Out of memory at rease shape !!!" (the typo is
  the original developer's) through the CRT output helper at 0x00042deb and calls
  exit(1) -- the failure path does not return.
  Writes a 6-byte shape header into the new buffer: 16-bit width 0x18, 16-bit
  height 0x18, 16-bit sprite count. Zero-fills the count * 0x240 pixel bytes that
  follow with the CRT memset at 0x00042cd0.
  Then loops i from 0 to count-1 calling
  fdps_cel_blit_sprite(sheet, i, dest_base, 0x18, 6, i * 0x18, 0, 0). The blitter
  computes dest_base + y * pitch + x, so pitch 0x18, x 6 and y i*0x18 resolve to
  dest_base + 6 + i * 0x240 -- x is being abused as a byte fudge to step over the
  6-byte header, not as a real column. Mode 0 means the plain unremapped,
  unflipped RLE variant, so each sprite is written out as a flat 24x24 block.
  Standard four-register prologue (EBX, ESI, EDI, EBP); EBX, ESI and EDI are
  saved and restored but never used. Every callee's arguments are pushed on the
  stack and cleaned by this function (ADD ESP,n), and this function itself takes
  no arguments and ends in a plain RET, matching the module default stack
  convention.

Parameters:
  None. The source sheet is read from the global at 0x00069c20.

Returns:
  Pointer to the newly allocated shape buffer: a 6-byte header (width 24,
  height 24, sprite count) followed by count consecutive 576-byte uncompressed
  24x24 sprites. Never returns NULL -- allocation failure exits the process.
  The caller owns the buffer.

=== 0002e689 [EOL]
Dead cdecl stack cleanup (ADD ESP,0x4) after a noreturn call to crt_exit; body of fdps_cel_expand_sheet_24x24, not a function entry.

=== 0002e70e [EOL]
2-byte alignment filler (8b c0, mov eax,eax) aligning next function to 0x2e710

=== 0002e710 [PLATE]
Renders the scaled (zoomable) battle map overview into an offscreen 320x200 buffer.

Algorithm:
Reads the current map dimensions from the map header pointed to by the global at 0x00069cb0: width = *(int16*)(hdr+7), height = *(int16*)(hdr+9). Converts the requested camera centre into a top-left world position by subtracting step*0x9c horizontally and step*0x60 vertically (0x9c=156 and 0x60=96 are half the 312x192 viewport), so the caller passes the point that should land at the centre of the view.

World coordinates are 1.12-style fixed point with 0xc00 units per map tile and 0x80 units per source pixel, i.e. every tile is 24x24 pixels (0xc00/0x80 = 24). The top-left position is split into tile index (world / 0xc00) and sub-tile remainder (world % 0xc00), with a manual floor correction that adds 0xc00 to the remainder and decrements the tile index when the remainder is negative, so negative camera positions still sample the correct tile.

The destination buffer is first cleared to colour 0 over its full 64000 bytes, then drawing starts at buffer + 0x504 (row 4, column 4) and advances by 0x140 (320) bytes per output row.

For each of 192 output rows the sub-tile Y remainder is converted to a source row offset ((rem >> 7) * 0x18) inside the tile bitmap. Rows whose tile Y is outside [0, height) are skipped, leaving the cleared background. Otherwise the tile bitmap pointer is fetched from the caller-supplied pointer table as table[tileY * 64 + tileX] (the table has a fixed stride of 64 entries per map row) and the row offset is added.

For each of 312 output columns one byte is copied from the tile bitmap at source column (subX >> 7), skipped when tile X is outside [0, width). After each column the sub-tile X remainder advances by step; when it reaches 0xc00 the tile X index advances, the remainder wraps by subtracting 0xc00, and the tile bitmap pointer is re-fetched from the table. The same wrap logic advances tile Y between rows. Tile X and its remainder are restored from the saved row-start values before each new row.

The step parameter is therefore the zoom factor in source-pixel fixed point: 0x80 gives 1:1, larger values zoom out, smaller values zoom in.

Parameters:
  dest       - destination 320x200 8bpp offscreen buffer, cleared to 0 first
  world_x    - camera centre X in 1/0x80-of-a-pixel fixed point (0xc00 per tile)
  world_y    - camera centre Y in the same fixed point
  step       - fixed-point source step per output pixel, i.e. the zoom factor
  tile_table - array of pointers to 24x24 tile bitmaps, indexed [tileY * 64 + tileX]

Returns:
  Nothing. EAX at RET holds the leftover loop counter and carries no meaning.

=== 0002e90c [EOL]
4-byte Watcom alignment NOP (lea eax,[eax+eiz*1+0x0]) padding to the 16-byte entry of the next function

=== 0002e910 [PLATE]
Applies already-triggered map cell state to the tile layer.

Algorithm:
Reads the current map header via the pointer at 0x00069cb0: width = word at +7, height = word at +9; the tile array is a width*height array of 16-bit tile indices starting at +0x0b. Reads a second, parallel map layer via the pointer at 0x00060148: its own width is the word at +7 and its byte array starts at +0x10.
Iterates y over [0,height) and x over [0,width). For each cell it calls the map cell query helper at 0x0002ba00 with (x, y), which publishes the cell record into the globals at 0x00069d00..0x00069d0f; this routine then uses the kind byte at 0x00069d08 and the cell id word at 0x00069d06.
A cell is acted on when (kind & 0x60) equals 0x20 or equals 0x60 and the byte table at 0x000640d8 indexed by the cell id is non-zero. Note that the two equality tests together are exactly the bit test (kind & 0x20), so the original source compared the masked kind against two named constants rather than testing one bit; keep both comparisons when rebuilding.
For an acted-on cell it increments the 16-bit tile index at tiles[y*width + x] by one and stores 0 into the second layer at layer2[y*layer2_width + x].
The table at 0x000640d8 is zero-filled in the image and is passed by address to the savegame reader and writer, so it is persistent per-cell trigger state; this routine is the pass that makes that state visible on the map after a load or a map (re)init.

Parameters:
None. All inputs come from the globals named above.

Returns:
Nothing.

=== 0002ea02 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov eax,eax, aligns next function to 0x2ea10

=== 0002ea10 [PLATE]
End the player phase once no friendly unit can still act.

Algorithm:
Sets a local 'all done' flag to 1, then walks the battle unit table: index 0 to DAT_00060150-1 (unit count), record base DAT_00069CD8, record stride 0x50 bytes. A record clears the flag when all three of these hold:
  byte +5 (status flags) has neither bit 0x80 nor bit 0x01 set, i.e. the unit is neither already finished nor removed
  byte +6 (side) equals 2, the player's side
  byte +0x26 equals 0, the per-unit acted marker
The loop always runs to completion; it does not break early. Afterwards, if the flag is still 1 -- no friendly unit remains actionable -- it calls FUN_0001E3F0, the phase-completion routine that the battle system menu also invokes after sweeping every remaining unit.

Parameters:
None. The prologue pushes EBX/ESI/EDI/EBP and reserves 0x18 bytes of locals; nothing at [EBP+0x14] or beyond is read, and every local is written before use. The decompiler's param_1/param_2/param_4/unaff_EBX are artifacts, as is the argument list it shows on the call to FUN_0001E3F0 -- the disassembly is a bare CALL with no preceding pushes.

Returns:
Nothing.

=== 0002eaa9 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0x0] (6-byte nop) + nop, aligning to 0002eab0

=== 0002eab0 [PLATE]
Advances the VGA palette colour-cycling animation for the current scene.

Algorithm:
1. Copies about twenty short byte ramps out of read-only tables at 0002b2a0..0002b4e9 onto the stack. The ramps come in R/G/B triples; every byte is a 6-bit VGA DAC component (observed range 0..63). Each ramp is (count + count/... ) bytes long so that a sliding window of `count` entries can start at any phase without wrapping: e.g. a 5-colour cycle stores 9 bytes, a 15-colour cycle stores 29 bytes.
2. Calls fdps_cd_music_repeat_poll (00030c50) as per-frame housekeeping.
3. Gates on the frame tick: if the last-serviced tick at 00069d20 already equals the current tick at 00069d64 the function returns without touching the palette, so the animation advances at most once per tick.
4. Switches on the scene / screen id at 00069cf4 (cases 0, 2, 8, 0x14, 0x15, 0x16, 0x17, 0x1a, 0x1c, 0x1d). Each case picks its own R/G/B ramp triple, its own DAC start index (0xf0, 0xf5, 0xf6 or 0xf8) and its own colour count (5, 7, 9, 10, 11 or 15).
5. Advances the shared phase counter at 00069d24 as (phase + 1) mod (count * 4) — the times-four factor makes the cycle advance one colour every four ticks — then derives the window offset as phase / 4 and calls FUN_0002f240(first_index, r + offset, g + offset, b + offset, count) with cdecl argument order (the caller does ADD ESP,0x14).
6. Records the serviced tick by storing 00069d64 into 00069d20.

Unrecognised scene ids fall through to the tick bookkeeping and change no colours.

Parameters: none. All inputs are globals: 00069cf4 scene id, 00069d20 last serviced tick, 00069d24 cycle phase, 00069d64 current frame tick.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0002eceb [EOL]
Unreachable duplicate JMP 0x0002f221 inside fdps_cycle_scene_palette; interior dead-code hole, no function entry here -- fold the 5 bytes into the existing function as unreachable code.

=== 0002ed0f [EOL]
Unreachable duplicate JMP 0002f221 inside fdps_cycle_scene_palette; disassemble into that function's body, do not create a function here.

=== 0002f232 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov eax,eax, aligning 0002f240 to 16.

=== 0002f2e4 [EOL]
Alignment filler: 12 zero bytes padding FUN_0002f240's body end up to the 16-byte-aligned entry of fdps_blit_rect.

=== 0002f2f0 [PLATE]
Strided rectangular block transfer for framebuffer/surface memory.

Algorithm:
Two modes selected by src_stride. When src_stride is zero the routine runs in fill mode: it treats src_or_fill as a byte value and calls memset(dst, src_or_fill, bytes_per_row) once per row, advancing dst by dst_stride after each row. When src_stride is non-zero it runs in copy mode: it calls memmove(dst, src, bytes_per_row) once per row, advancing src by src_stride and dst by dst_stride after each row. Both modes loop exactly rows times with a signed comparison, so a rows value of zero or less transfers nothing. No clipping, no bounds check and no transparency test is performed; the caller is responsible for the geometry. The row counter is kept in a stack local and the working src/dst cursors are stack copies of the incoming pointers, so the caller's values are not disturbed.

Parameters:
  src_or_fill  - in copy mode the source base pointer; in fill mode the byte value used to fill every row (only the low 8 bits reach memset)
  src_stride   - bytes added to the source cursor per row; zero selects fill mode
  dst          - destination base pointer
  dst_stride   - bytes added to the destination cursor per row
  bytes_per_row- number of bytes transferred per row
  rows         - number of rows to transfer; signed, values <= 0 do nothing

Returns:
  Nothing.

Alignment padding: 3-byte NOP form 8d 40 00 (lea eax,[eax+0]) aligning FUN_0002f390 to 16 bytes.

=== 0002f40f [EOL]
Single-byte 0x90 NOP alignment pad before FUN_0002f410.

=== 0002f6c7 [EOL]
Alignment padding: multi-byte NOP forms lea eax,[eax+0] + lea edx,[edx+0], aligning FUN_0002f6d0 to 16 bytes.

=== 0002f6de [EOL]
2-byte filler (mov eax,eax) aligning the switch jump table at 0002f6e0 to a dword boundary

=== 0002fb77 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 8d 80 00 00 00 00 + 8d 52 00, 9 bytes to 16-byte boundary at 0002fb80

=== 0002fb80 [PLATE]
Mosaic screen transition: copies the source surface to the destination in block-sized rectangles, visiting them in a shuffled order so the image appears in random patches.

Algorithm:
1. Allocates grid_cols*grid_rows entries of 4 bytes (two int16 fields: column, row) via the CRT allocator and fills it with every (col,row) pair of the phase grid.
2. Shuffles the table in place: for every index i it draws two rand() values and forms j = rand()%grid_cols + grid_cols*(rand()%grid_rows), then swaps entry i with entry j. This is a random-swap shuffle, not a Fisher-Yates pass.
3. Computes the block counts: blocks_x = ceil((width/block_w)/grid_cols), blocks_y = ceil((height/block_h)/grid_rows). Each shuffled entry therefore owns a lattice of blocks spaced grid_cols apart.
4. For each shuffled entry (bx,by), walks that lattice. Row index is y = block_h*(j*grid_cols + by); column index is x = bx + i*grid_cols. A block is drawn only when x < width and y < height. Source address is src + y*src_pitch + x*block_w; destination address is dst + y*dst_pitch + (dst_pitch ? x*block_w : 0). Each block is handed to fdps_blit_rect with block_w by block_h.
5. After finishing one shuffled entry it calls the delay/present thunk at 0003d370 with the delay argument, so one entry of the table equals one visible step of the transition.
6. Frees the table before returning.

Note the bound test on the horizontal axis compares a block-column index against the pixel width, while the vertical test compares a pixel row against the pixel height. The horizontal test is therefore far looser than the vertical one; the real bound comes from the blocks_x loop count.

Parameters:
  dst        destination surface base address
  dst_pitch  destination bytes per row; zero suppresses the horizontal destination offset entirely
  src        source surface base address
  src_pitch  source bytes per row
  width      source width in pixels
  height     source height in pixels
  grid_cols  number of phase columns; also the horizontal stride between blocks of one phase
  grid_rows  number of phase rows
  block_w    block width in pixels
  block_h    block height in pixels
  delay      value passed to the per-step delay/present thunk at 0003d370

Returns:
  Nothing.

=== 0002fe31 [EOL]
Watcom 16-byte alignment padding between fdps_transition_random_blocks and FUN_0002fe40

=== 00030006 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: 6-byte + 4-byte multi-byte NOP forms aligning the next function to 16 bytes.

=== 00030010 [PLATE]
Alpha-blends a single constant palette colour over an 8bpp source rectangle.

Algorithm:
blend_table is a [18][256] array of dwords holding each palette entry pre-scaled by a weight, with the three colour channels packed one per byte (channel n in bits 8n+4..8n+7 before the shift below). Rows 0..8 hold weights 0..8; rows 9..17 (dword offset 0x900 = 9*256) hold the complementary weights. To keep the row index inside 0..8 the function folds alpha: for alpha <= 8 the constant colour reads row alpha and the source pixels read row 9+alpha; for alpha > 8 alpha is replaced by 16-alpha, the constant colour reads row 9+alpha' and the source pixels read row alpha'. Either way the constant colour ends up weighted by the caller's alpha and the source pixel by 16-alpha.
The scaled constant colour is fetched once before the loops. For each pixel the scaled source colour is added to it, the sum is shifted right by 4 (dividing out the /16 of the weights) and masked with 0x000F0F0F, leaving 4 bits per channel at bits 0..3, 8..11 and 16..19. Those are folded into a 12-bit index by (v & 0xFFFF) | (v >> 12), giving channel0 in bits 0..3, channel2 in bits 4..7 and channel1 in bits 8..11. That index selects a palette byte from the 4096-entry inverse-palette table rgb444_lut, which is stored to the destination.
Rows advance by src_stride and dst_stride; no clipping, no transparency test, every pixel in the rectangle is written.

Parameters:
src         - source 8bpp pixels, top-left of the rectangle
src_stride  - bytes to add to src per row
dst         - destination 8bpp pixels, top-left of the rectangle
dst_stride  - bytes to add to dst per row
width       - pixels per row
height      - number of rows
blend_table - base of the [18][256] dword weighted-colour table
rgb444_lut  - base of the 4096-entry packed-RGB to palette-index table
tint_color  - palette index of the constant colour being blended in
alpha       - blend weight of tint_color, 0..16 (0 = source only, 16 = tint only)

Returns:
Nothing.

Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] + lea edx,[edx+eiz*1+0], aligns next function to 0x30120.

=== 000304dc [EOL]
8d 44 20 00, Watcom 4-byte NOP form aligning the next entry fdps_audio_init to 000304e0

=== 000304e0 [PLATE]
Audio subsystem bring-up.

Algorithm:
1. Call AIL startup (0003d718) unconditionally.
2. Clear the two driver-present flags (0x00069d72, 0x00069d71) and set the audio-enabled flag 0x00069d70 to 1.
3. Install the MDI/music driver (0004044c) and store the driver pointer in 0x00069d6c. If it is non-NULL, set flag 0x00069d72 and allocate a single sequence handle (0004067d) into 0x00069d60.
4. Install the DIG/digital driver (0003ea65) and store the driver pointer in 0x00069d68. If it is non-NULL, set flag 0x00069d71 and allocate eight sample handles (0003ecaa) into the array at 0x00069d30.
5. Tail-call the follow-up audio setup routine 000307b0, forwarding this function's single argument.

Both driver installs are optional: a NULL return leaves the corresponding flag at 0 and the game runs without that half of the audio system. The loop counter body contains a dead load of the index, which is normal for the -4s / unoptimised build.

Parameters:
  param1 - opaque value forwarded unchanged to 000307b0; not inspected here.

Returns:
  void.

=== 00030597 [EOL]

=== 000305a0 [PLATE]
Tear down the Miles AIL audio stack on the way out of the game.

Algorithm:
  The entire body is the Watcom four-push prologue, a single call to
  AIL_shutdown (0003d8b2), and the matching epilogue. Nothing else: no
  argument is passed, no global is touched, no return value is inspected.

  Identity rests on three things rather than on anything the body itself
  says. The callee at 0003d8b2 is the Miles public API entry AIL_shutdown,
  fixed by the string "AIL_shutdown()\n" at 000623a5 and the standard Miles
  log-wrapper shape around it. This function is the second step of main's
  three-call teardown sequence, between fdps_shutdown_free_resources
  (00029440) and fdps_cd_stop_audio (0003c4a7). And it is the exact
  counterpart of fdps_audio_init (000304e0), which is the one-time AIL
  startup called from main's init sequence.

  A wrapper this thin exists because the game calls AIL through its own
  one-per-operation shims rather than calling the library directly; the same
  pattern appears throughout the audio subsystem.

Parameters:
  None. No register is read before being written and no stack slot is
  accessed.

Returns:
  Nothing. AIL_shutdown's result, if any, is discarded.

=== 00030790 [PLATE]
Periodic timer tick callback.

Algorithm: Increments the global 32-bit tick counter at 00069d64, then calls the per-tick service routine FUN_00042cf8 and returns. The tick counter is read (never written) by roughly fifty game functions -- title screen, game over, view rendering, and many battle/menu routines -- which sample it to build delay and frame-pacing loops, so this handler is the single writer that makes those loops advance. The function's address is taken and passed to fdps_audio_timer_install (address reference at 000307bc, stored at 000307c1), which is the only reference to it: nothing calls it directly, it is invoked from the installed timer hook. The body touches no parameters and no registers beyond the standard PUSH ESI/EDI/EBP frame; the SUB ESP,0x0 is Watcom's empty-locals prologue.

Parameters: None.

Returns: Nothing.

=== 000307aa [EOL]
Alignment padding: one 6-byte lea eax,[eax+0x0] NOP aligning fdps_audio_timer_install to 0x10.

=== 000307b0 [PLATE]
Installs and starts the audio service timer.

Algorithm:
  1. Calls the timer-registration routine FUN_0003e196 with the address of the
     tick callback FUN_00030790 (which bumps the counter at 0x00069d64 and then
     calls the audio service routine FUN_00042cf8). The returned timer handle is
     stored in the global at 0x00069d50.
  2. If the handle is -1 the registration failed; the fatal/report routine
     FUN_00042deb is called with the literal " Timer fail !!!\n" at 0x00061ea8.
     Execution is not stopped locally - the function falls through regardless.
  3. Calls FUN_0003e3ea(handle, rate) to program the timer with the rate value
     passed in by the caller.
  4. Calls FUN_0003e5b3(handle) to start the timer.

All three timer routines are reached with arguments pushed on the stack and the
caller cleaning up (ADD ESP,n after each CALL), i.e. the vendor sound library's
stack convention.

Parameters:
  rate - timer rate/period value handed straight to the timer programming call;
         the only stack slot this function reads ([EBP+0x14]).

Returns:
  Nothing. The timer handle is left in the global at 0x00069d50 for later use.

=== 00030c50 [PLATE]
Periodic CD background-music keep-alive, polled from the game loops.

Algorithm:
  Compares the cached tick value at 0x00069d74 with the live timer tick at
  0x00069d64 and returns immediately when they are equal, so the body runs at
  most once per timer tick regardless of how often the caller polls.
  On a new tick it increments the tick accumulator at 0x00060170. When the
  accumulator reaches 0x4b (75 ticks) it performs the actual check: if a track
  is selected (0x00069d54 != -1) and the CD audio enable flag at 0x00060008 is
  non-zero, it queries playback state via FUN_0003c6e8; a non-zero 16-bit
  result means the track is no longer playing, in which case FUN_0003c85b is
  called with (track index + 1) to start the track again. The accumulator is
  then reset to zero. Finally the cached tick is refreshed from the live tick.
  The selected-track global holds a zero-based index; the play routine takes a
  one-based CD track number, hence the +1.

Parameters:
  None. All state is read from and written to globals.

Returns:
  Nothing.

=== 00030cc0 [PLATE]
Verify the correct game CD is inserted for a chapter, then start its CD audio track.

Algorithm:
1. Copy a 60-byte constant table from 000304a0 onto the stack. It is 30 chapter rows of 2 bytes; the row is the chapter, the column is the track slot, and the byte is a zero-based CD audio track index.
2. Stop any playing CD audio (fdps_cd_stop_audio at 0003c4a7).
3. Loop until the inserted disc is acceptable:
   - Uninstall the keyboard ISR (00056818) and drain pending input (00043570 / 00043587).
   - Build "%s\\Pack.vfs" from the CD-ROM root path global at 000643c4 (00042d41) and probe it (00042438). A non-zero result means the archive could not be opened, i.e. no usable disc.
   - On success, load the "Pass.Dat" entry through fdps_vfs_load_entry and take its first byte minus '0' as the disc number (1 or 2).
   - Chapters 0..17 live on disc 1, chapters 18 (0x12) and above on disc 2. If the probe failed, or the disc number does not match the chapter, draw the matching prompt string (message id 0x223 for "insert disc 2", 0x222 for "insert disc 1", followed by id 0x22a) via 0001ff60 at 320x208, wait 20000 ticks (0003d370), tear the prompt down (00020820) and retry.
   - Otherwise set the done flag and leave the loop.
4. Reinstall the keyboard ISR (fdps_install_keyboard_isr).
5. If the CD audio enable flag at 00060008 is clear, store -1 into the current-track global at 00069d54 and stop CD audio. Otherwise read table[chapter * 2 + slot], store it at 00069d54, and start playback of track value + 1 (0003c85b).

Parameters:
  chapter - chapter index, 0..29; also selects the required disc (>= 18 means disc 2).
  slot    - which of the chapter's two track entries to play, 0 or 1.

Returns:
  Nothing. Does not return until an acceptable disc is present.

=== 00030f32 [EOL]
Alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / mov eax,eax, aligning fdps_play_movie to 16 bytes.

=== 00030f40 [PLATE]
Plays one full-screen FMV sequence by name.

Algorithm:
1. Calls 0003d8b2 and 00056818 to quiesce the current display/audio state, then spins on 00043570 / 00043587 until the pending playback-side work drains.
2. Calls fdps_cd_stop_audio, then fades the active palette at DAT_000643bc down by (-64,-64,-64) over entries 0..255 via 00022f40.
3. Formats three paths with sprintf (00042d41) into local buffers, all rooted at the base directory string at DAT_000643e8:
     [ebp-0x3c] = "%s\\fd.exe"      (player/module path, no extra argument)
     [ebp-0x28] = "%s\\%s.Vid"      (video stream, second %s = movie_name)
     [ebp-0x14] = "%s\\%s.Aud"      (audio stream, second %s = movie_name)
4. Invokes the playback entry 000435a2 with (0, fd_exe_path, fd_exe_path, vid_path, aud_path, 0).
5. On return, zero-fills 64000 bytes at 0x000a0000 (mode 13h framebuffer) via 00042cd0, re-programs the palette to all-zero through 00022f40, calls 000567f0 to tear down the playback display state, and finally calls fdps_audio_init(0x19) to bring music back up.

Parameters:
  movie_name - pointer to the base name of the movie, substituted for the second %s of both the .Vid and .Aud path templates. Passed on the stack at [ebp+0x14].

Returns:
  Nothing. Returns after the movie has finished and the screen has been cleared.

=== 00031210 [PLATE]
Runs the village phase between chapters.

Algorithm:
1. Poll the background service at 0x000567b3, then bail out immediately with 0 if the global abort flag at 0x000643eb is set.
2. Test the current chapter number at 0x00069cf4. Chapters 16, 17, 21, 22 and 26+ have no village: those call the fade/transition helper at 0x00030bf0 with -1 and, unless the suppress flag at 0x00060131 is set, clear VGA memory at 0xa0000 (64000 bytes), run the palette routine at 0x00022f40 with the palette at 0x000643bc, allocate and clear a 64000-byte offscreen buffer, present it via 0x000241e0 and free it. Control then falls through to step 6.
3. Otherwise set the in-village flag at 0x00060070, load "ShopWin.Cel" from the "MISC_VFS" archive via the resource loader at 0x0002a140, cache the handle in 0x00069d84, run the village setup at 0x00031540 and fade in via 0x00030bf0 with mode 2.
4. Menu loop: allocate a 64000-byte page, load "Village.cel" from "MISC_VFS", blit it to the page at pitch 0x140 via 0x0002dba0, free the background, draw the cursor highlight at the coordinate pair for the current selection (x table at 0x00060174, y table at 0x0006018c) via 0x00031780 with flag 1, run the menu input handler at 0x00031bc0 which updates the selection in place, erase the highlight with flag 0, free the page.
5. Dispatch on the selection through the jump table at 0x00031328: 1 -> 0x00035cc0 (which forces the selection back to 0 for chapters that have no village), 2 -> 0x00035aa0, 3 -> 0x00035860, 4 -> 0x00035fd0, 5 -> 0x00036210. Poll 0x000567b3 and repeat while the selection is non-zero and no abort is pending. On exit free the two cached cel handles, clear the in-village flag at 0x00060070 and the state words at 0x00060150 and 0x00069cd8.
6. Save and clear the input-enable byte at 0x00060158, fade out via 0x00030bf0 with -1, and unless an abort is pending call the chapter script entry taken from the per-chapter function pointer table at 0x00060074 indexed by the chapter number. Restore 0x00060158, clear 0x00060131, and call 0x00030cc0 with the chapter number and 0.

Parameters: none.

Returns: the menu selection left over when the loop exited, which is 0 on a normal exit and 0 when the abort flag was already set on entry.

=== 00031325 [EOL]
Alignment padding: 3-byte NOP form lea eax,[eax+0], aligns to 0x31328.

=== 0003153a [EOL]

=== 00031540 [PLATE]
Loads (and reloads) the per-chapter resources used by the field/village phase.

Algorithm:
1. If the cached chapter text buffer at 0x00060124 is non-NULL, free it.
2. sprintf a name with the format string "fdetxt%02d.txt" at 0x00061f10 using chapter index [0x00069cf4] + 1, load that member out of the archive named by "Field.vfs" (0x000600f8) via FUN_0002a140, and store the returned buffer in 0x00060124.
3. sprintf a name with "Shop%02d.dat" (0x00061f20) using chapter index [0x00069cf4] -- note: no +1 here, unlike the text file -- load it the same way and store the buffer in 0x00069d80.
4. If [0x00060150] is non-zero and the cached roster pointer [0x00069cd8] differs from the live roster base [0x00064108], free the stale roster copy.
5. Snapshot the live roster: [0x00060150] = roster count [0x00064114], [0x00069cd8] = roster base [0x00064108].
6. For i in 0 .. [0x00069cdc)-1, free the three parallel per-entry buffer arrays at 0x00069cb0, 0x00069c20 and 0x00069c98, then reset the entry count [0x00069cdc] to 0.
7. If the flag [0x00069cf0] is set, free the buffer held in [0x00060138] and clear the flag.
8. Open "ICON.CEL" (0x00061f34) in mode "rb" (0x00061f30) via the CRT fopen at 0x0004265e.
9. For each of the [0x00064114] roster records (stride 0x50 bytes from [0x00064108]), read the icon id at record offset +7 and pass it with the open stream to FUN_00023050, which decodes that icon.
10. Close the stream via the CRT fclose at 0x000428be.

Parameters: none. All inputs are globals: the chapter index at 0x00069cf4, the roster base/count at 0x00064108/0x00064114, and the cached-resource slots listed above.

Returns: nothing. The loaded buffers are published through the globals 0x00060124 (chapter text) and 0x00069d80 (shop table).

=== 00031bc0 [PLATE]
Village signboard menu loop.

Algorithm:
1. Copies three 9-byte VGA DAC ramp tables (R at 0x31064, G at 0x3106d, B at 0x31076) into locals; they drive the pulsing highlight colour.
2. Clamps the global scene index at 0x000601a4 against the count at 0x00064114, then loads the signboard cel "CanBan.cel" from the "MISC_VFS" archive (FUN_0002a140). The handle is freed on exit.
3. Each iteration: draws the village backdrop (FUN_00030c50), reads a keyboard scancode (FUN_000567be, masked to 8 bits). Scancodes >= 0x7F are ignored. FUN_000357a0 acts as a hotkey/abort filter: when it returns non-zero the selection is forced to 5 and the selection-change handler FUN_00031f60 runs.
   - 0x4B (left) / 0x48 (up): plays "Beep.wav" (FUN_0002a1f0), selection = (selection + 4) % 5, calls FUN_00031f60.
   - 0x4D (right) / 0x50 (down): plays "Beep.wav", selection = (selection + 1) % 5, calls FUN_00031f60.
   - 0x1C (Enter) / 0x39 (Space): sets the exit flag.
   - 0x0F (Tab): advances the scene index at 0x000601a4 modulo the count at 0x00064114.
4. Picks an animation cell from the frame counter (frame/5 & 3, with 3 folded back to 1), resolves the sprite through the table at 0x00060138 offset by scene index * 0x30, allocates a 64000-byte off-screen page (FUN_0003d375) and fills it with the caller-supplied background image (FUN_0003d514, 320x200).
5. Blits the animated figure (FUN_000568db) at the position given by the x table at 0x00060174 and the y table at 0x0006018C indexed by the current selection, then draws the signboard text twice through FUN_0002dba0 (once for entry 6, once for the current selection, i.e. the highlighted line).
6. Waits for vertical retrace on port 0x3DA (bit 3), then writes DAC entries 0xF0..0xF4 through ports 0x3C8/0x3C9 using the ramp tables phase-shifted by (frame/3) % 5, waits for retrace to end, copies the off-screen page to 0xA0000, spins until the timer tick at 0x00069D64 changes, and frees the page.

Parameters:
  background - pointer to a 64000-byte (320x200) background image copied into the off-screen page every frame.
  selection  - in/out pointer to the current menu selection (0..4, or 5 when the hotkey filter fires); updated in place and read by the caller after return.

Returns:
  Nothing. The cel handle and the off-screen page are released before returning.

=== 00035860 [PLATE]
Village item screen driver.

Algorithm:
1. Copies a 5-dword menu descriptor table from DAT_00031180 into a local array (the menu entry ids 0x24, 0x14, 0x15, 0x04, 0x10).
2. Loads asset "Item.cel" from container "MISC.VFS" (FUN_0002a140), allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) surface (FUN_0003d375), publishes it in DAT_00063fb4, blits the cel into it with pitch 0x140 (FUN_0002dba0), then frees the cel (FUN_0003d478).
3. Runs the screen transition FUN_00031780(surface, 0x9f, 0x63, 1), presents the surface (FUN_00032570), restores the 0xa8208 region (FUN_00032370) and draws the header text (message id 0x1f4 via FUN_0001ff60).
4. Loops: FUN_000323e0(menu_table, 5, &selection) reads a selection; a jump table at 0x35948 dispatches case 0 (redraw plus message id 4), case 1 -> FUN_00033b80(surface, 0), case 2 -> FUN_00034000(surface), case 3 -> FUN_00034210(surface), case 4 -> FUN_00033f80(surface). After each entry the screen is re-presented and message id 0x1f5 redrawn; selection == -1 leaves the loop.
5. On exit runs the closing transition FUN_00031780(surface, 0x9f, 0x63, 0) and frees the surface (FUN_0003d478).

Parameters: none. State comes from the globals DAT_000643c4 / DAT_00060124 (text sources) and the menu table at DAT_00031180.

Returns: nothing.

=== 00035aa0 [PLATE]
Village church facility screen (one of the village sub-screens dispatched from fdps_run_village_phase).

Algorithm:
1. Copies a 16-byte menu descriptor {0x24, 0x12, 4, 0x10} from the read-only table at 0x00031194 onto the stack; the fields read as x=36, y=18, item_count=4, line_height=16.
2. Opens resource "Church.cel" out of container "MISC.VFS" (FUN_0002a140), allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) screen buffer (FUN_0003d375), publishes the buffer pointer in the global at 0x00063fb4, decodes the cel into the buffer (FUN_0002dba0, stride 0x140), then frees the cel data (FUN_0003d478).
3. Presents the backdrop with FUN_00031780(buffer, 0x9f, 0x63, 1) - a fade/transfer centred on (159, 99) with the enable flag set - and does an initial page/cursor update (FUN_00032570) plus a full-width copy of the 0x000A8208 region (FUN_00032370) and a text/dialog draw (FUN_0001ff60, id 0x200).
4. Main loop: while the selection variable is not -1, run the menu widget FUN_000323e0(descriptor, 4, &selection) and switch on the result via the jump table at 0x00035b84:
     case 0 - no handler; only toggles the display twice and redraws through the global at 0x00060124 with id 7.
     case 1 - FUN_000345a0(buffer)
     case 2 - FUN_00034210(buffer)
     case 3 - FUN_00033f80(buffer)
   After the handler, selection == -1 leaves the display enabled and exits the loop; otherwise the backdrop and the id 0x201 text are redrawn and the loop repeats.
5. On exit, FUN_00031780(buffer, 0x9f, 0x63, 0) fades the screen back out and the 64000-byte buffer is freed.

Parameters: none. The function reads nothing above the frame pointer; all state comes from the resource files and the globals at 0x00063fb4, 0x000643c4 and 0x00060124.

Returns: nothing.

=== 00035cc0 [PLATE]
Village bar/tavern screen.

Algorithm:
1. Copies a 4-entry menu descriptor table from 0x000311a4 (0x24, 9, 10, 8) into a local array.
2. Loads "BarShop.cel" out of the "MISC.VFS" container (FUN_0002a140), allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) backbuffer via the CRT allocator, decodes the cel into it (FUN_0002dba0, pitch 0x140), publishes the buffer in the global at 0x00063fb4, then frees the raw file image.
3. Fades the screen in (FUN_00031780 with flag 1), starts the screen's audio/entry hook (FUN_00036460), shows the cursor, uploads the palette at 0xa8208 and prints the bar greeting (FUN_0001ff60, text id 0x200).
4. Loops: FUN_000323e0 drives the 4-entry menu and returns the picked index in a local; a switch dispatches index 0..3. Index 0 prints a canned message (text id 6). Index 1 enters FUN_000241e0. Index 2 enters FUN_00024490 and, when it returns 1, calls FUN_00031540(1) and ends the loop. Index 3 asks a confirmation (text id 0x1f0) via FUN_00017990; on a zero answer it sets the byte flag at 0x000643eb, prints text id 0x1f1, delays ~300 ticks and ends the loop.
5. Each iteration that does not end the loop hides the cursor, reprints the greeting (text id 0x201) and restores the palette; the sentinel -1 in the selection variable terminates the loop.
6. On exit fades out (FUN_00031780 with flag 0) and frees the backbuffer.

Parameters: none.
Returns: nothing.

=== 00036210 [PLATE]
Village-phase Secret screen menu loop.

Algorithm:
1. Copies a 6-entry menu descriptor table from 0x000311c8 (values 0x24, 0x14, 0x15, 0x04, 0x06, 0x10) into a stack copy at [ebp-0x24]; the loop mutates the copy so the table stays pristine.
2. Loads "Secret.cel" out of the "MISC.VFS" container (0x0002a140), allocates a 64000-byte (320x200) backbuffer via 0x0003d375, publishes the pointer to the global at 0x00063fb4, decodes the CEL into the buffer with pitch 0x140 (0x0002dba0), then frees the raw CEL data (0x0003d478).
3. Fades the screen in through 0x00031780 with centre (0x9f, 0x63) and direction 1, presents the buffer (0x00032570, mode 0), refreshes the 0x140-wide overlay at 0xa8208 (0x00032370), and draws the prompt with message id 0x202 (0x0001ff60, using the global at 0x000643c4).
4. Loops while the selection is not -1: 0x000323e0 reads a selection from the 6-entry table into the local, a jump table at 0x000362f8 dispatches cases 0..5 -- case 0 presents mode 2 then mode 0 and draws message id 8 with the global at 0x00060124, cases 1..5 call 0x00033b80 (with sub-mode 2), 0x00034000, 0x00034210, 0x00034420 and 0x00033f80 respectively, each taking only the backbuffer. Selections above 5 fall through to the tail.
5. Tail of each iteration: if the selection is -1 the screen is presented in mode 1 and the loop exits; otherwise it is presented in mode 0, message id 0x1f5 is drawn and the overlay at 0xa8208 is refreshed, then control returns to the top of the loop.
6. On exit, fades out with 0x00031780 (centre 0x9f, 0x63, direction 0) and frees the backbuffer.

Parameters: none. The function reads no incoming arguments; Ghidra's param_1/param_2/unaff_EBX are decompiler artefacts.

Returns: nothing. The selected backbuffer pointer is also left in the global at 0x00063fb4 while the screen is live.

=== 00036b55 [EOL]

=== 00036b60 [PLATE]
Sets the low nibble of the byte at offset 0x34 for every record in a closed index range of the global 0x50-byte object table.

Algorithm:
  i = first_index
  while (i <= last_index) {
      rec = object_at(i);            /* FUN_0002d210: *(char**)0x00069cd8 + i * 0x50 */
      rec[0x34] = (rec[0x34] & 0xF0) | value;
  }
  The high nibble of rec[0x34] is preserved; only the low nibble is replaced.
  The loop is inclusive of last_index, so first_index == last_index touches exactly
  one record. If first_index > last_index nothing is written.
  All three observed call sites pass value = 0, i.e. they clear the low nibble.
  Only the low byte of the third stack slot is read (register DH).

Parameters:
  [EBP+0x14] int first_index  - first object index, inclusive
  [EBP+0x18] int last_index   - last object index, inclusive
  [EBP+0x1C] unsigned char value - value merged into the low nibble of field 0x34

Returns:
  Nothing. Arguments are passed on the stack and removed by the caller (ADD ESP,0xC).

=== 00039990 [PLATE]
Look up a named entry in an archive directory table.

Algorithm:
The entry count is the single byte at the start of the directory block (dir[0]).
For each index i from 0 to count-1 the routine copies 13 (0xd) bytes from
dir + i*0x1a + 0x11 -- the name field of a 26-byte directory entry -- into a
16-byte stack buffer via the 3-argument copy helper at 0x000435bc, passes the
caller-supplied name through the helper at 0x000435f3 (which returns a string
pointer), and compares the two with the compare helper at 0x00042fe0. A zero
result means a match: the loop stores i and returns it. If the loop runs off
the end without a match the result stays at its initial -1.
The search index is kept in a local and returned unchanged; there is no early
exit other than the match, and the comparison is done on the raw stored name
with no case folding of the table side.

Parameters:
  name - pointer to the name being looked up; passed through the helper at
         0x000435f3 before comparison (stack slot EBP+0x14)
  dir  - pointer to the directory block: byte 0 is the entry count, entry i
         has stride 0x1a and its 13-byte name field at +0x11
         (stack slot EBP+0x18)

Returns:
  Zero-based index of the matching directory entry, or -1 when no entry
  matches.

=== 00039a13 [EOL]
Watcom alignment padding: lea eax,[eax+0] / lea edx,[edx+0] / nop, 13 bytes to 16-byte boundary

=== 00039a20 [PLATE]
Watcom prologue 53 56 57 55 89 e5 / sub esp,0x20; cdecl, two stack args (ebp+0x14 name, ebp+0x18 table); scans a byte-counted array of 26-byte entries, copies 13 bytes from entry+0x11 into a local buffer via 000435bc, transforms the name arg via 000435f3, compares via 00042fe0, returns dword at entry+0x1e on match else -1
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 00039aaa [EOL]
Watcom 6-byte NOP (lea eax,[eax+0x0]) aligning fdps_vfs_open to 16 bytes

=== 00039ab0 [PLATE]
Opens a VFS container and builds its in-memory directory handle.

Algorithm:
  1. fopen(path, "rb"). Return NULL if it fails.
  2. fseek to offset 7, fread a u32 entry_count.
  3. fseek to offset 5, fread a u16 table_offset (later sign-extended when used as a seek target, so the usable range is 0..0x7FFF).
  4. Allocate 0x11 + entry_count * 0x1A bytes for the handle. If the allocation fails, still close the file and return NULL.
  5. memset the first 0x11 bytes of the handle to zero, store entry_count at offset 0, and memcpy strlen(path) bytes of the container path to offset 4 (the zeroed header leaves it NUL terminated for names up to 12 characters).
  6. fseek to table_offset and fread entry_count entries of 0x1A bytes each into handle+0x11.
  7. fclose and return the handle.

The container magic "VFS", the version word, the 24-byte signature, the duplicated per-entry size and the reserved byte are never examined, so a non-VFS file is not rejected here.

Parameters:
  path - NUL terminated container file name, opened through the C runtime, no directory search of its own.

Returns:
  Pointer to the allocated container handle {u32 entry_count; char path[13]; entry table of entry_count * 0x1A bytes}, or NULL if the file could not be opened or the handle could not be allocated.

=== 00039bca [EOL]
Alignment padding: 6-byte lea eax,[eax+0x0] NOP between fdps_vfs_open and fdps_vfs_load_file

=== 00039bd0 [PLATE]
Loads one named entry out of a VFS archive into a freshly allocated buffer.

Algorithm:
1. Calls the VFS name lookup helper at 00039990 with (name, vfs) to get the entry index; if it returns -1 prints "Can't find the string: %s" and returns NULL.
2. Copies 13 bytes of the archive path stored at vfs+4 into a local buffer (strncpy) and opens it with fopen(path, "rb"); on failure prints "Can't open the source VFS_file: %s" and returns NULL.
3. Reads the entry's byte length from the directory record at vfs + 0x1e + index * 0x1a (record field +0) and asks the allocator at 0003d375 for that many bytes; on failure prints "Can't allocate memory for VFS_file: %s(%dbytes)", closes the file and returns NULL.
4. Reads the entry's file offset from field +9 of the same record, fseek(fp, offset, SEEK_SET), fread(buf, length, 1, fp), then fclose(fp).
5. Returns the allocated buffer (NULL on any of the error paths above).

Directory layout implied by the arithmetic: a 0x1e byte header (archive path string at +4) followed by 0x1a byte records, each holding the entry length at +0 and the entry offset at +9.

Parameters:
  name - entry name to look up inside the archive; also used in the error messages.
  vfs  - pointer to the loaded VFS descriptor (header plus directory records).

Returns: pointer to the newly allocated buffer holding the entry's contents, or NULL if the entry was not found, the archive could not be opened, or the allocation failed. The caller owns the buffer.

=== 0003bade [PLATE]
Allocates the DOS real-mode communication buffers used by the CD-ROM (MSCDEX) driver layer.

Algorithm:
1. Zero the 12-byte struct SREGS at 0x00069df0 with memset.
2. Fill the input REGS at 0x00069dc8 with AX = 0x0100 and BX = 0x0020, then call int386x(0x31, in, out, sregs). DPMI function 0100h allocates 0x20 paragraphs (512 bytes) of DOS memory below 1MB; the returned AX is the real-mode segment and DX the protected-mode selector (the selector is discarded).
3. Store the first block's real-mode segment as a word at 0x00069e54 and its flat linear address (segment << 4) at 0x00069de8. Under DOS/4GW the first megabyte is identity mapped, so segment << 4 is directly dereferenceable.
4. Repeat the identical DPMI 0100h call for a second 512-byte block; store its segment shifted left 16 bits at 0x00069da8 (pre-packed seg:0000 real-mode far pointer for the MSCDEX request header transfer address field) and its flat linear address (segment << 4) at 0x00069da4.

The input REGS block is not re-initialised before the second call because int386x leaves the caller's input structure untouched.

No error handling: a failed DPMI allocation leaves the carry flag set and garbage in AX, and the resulting addresses are used unchecked by the CD driver functions.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing. Results are published through the globals at 0x00069da4, 0x00069da8, 0x00069de8 and 0x00069e54.

=== 0003bb7d [PLATE]
Sends the currently prepared MSCDEX request header to the CD-ROM device driver.

Algorithm:
Clears the SREGS block at 0x00069df0 (12 bytes) and the DPMI real-mode call structure at 0x00069e22 (0x32 bytes). Fills the real-mode structure with EAX=0x1510 (MSCDEX Send Device Request), ECX = the CD-ROM subunit number held in the byte at 0x00069dfe, EBX=0 and ES = the real-mode segment of the request header held in the word at 0x00069e54, so that the driver receives ES:BX pointing at the request header. It then fills the protected-mode input REGS block at 0x00069dc8 with EAX=0x0300 (DPMI Simulate Real Mode Interrupt), BL=0x2f (interrupt 2Fh), BH=0, ECX=0 (no words copied from the protected-mode stack) and EDI = 0x00069e22, sets SREGS.es to DS, and calls int86x(0x31, &inregs, &outregs, &sregs). If the carry flag in the output REGS (0x00069dc4) is set, it prints "DEVICE REQUEST FAILED!!!" and returns anyway; the MSCDEX status word in the request header is not inspected here.

Parameters:
None. Every input is a file-scope global: the request header itself and its real-mode segment at 0x00069e54, and the CD-ROM subunit number at 0x00069dfe. Callers fill the request header before calling.

Returns:
Nothing. Failure is only reported on stdout; the caller must read the request header status field itself.

=== 0003bce2 [PLATE]
push 0x38 / call 0004361a (Watcom __STK stack probe), sub esp,0x28, fills a 0x1e-byte DOS device request header on the stack (byte 0x1e, 0x00, cmd 0x03, dword from [00069da8], word 6), calls 000435bc three times with (dst, src, len), calls fdps_cd_device_request at 0003bb7d, stores the request status word to [00069e20], add esp,0x28, ret at 0003bd98
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003bd99 [PLATE]
