Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003be36 [PLATE]
push 0x28 / call 0004361a, sub esp,0x18, writes 0x03 through the pointer arg at [esp+0x1c], fills the same descriptor (byte 0x18, 0x00, 0x0c, word 9), calls 000435bc twice and fdps_cd_device_request; ends at 0003be9f with 'movzx eax,byte ptr [esp]; push eax' and falls through into the shared CD epilogue FUN_0003bea0, which is a tail transfer, not an unterminated body
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003bec1 [PLATE]
single function: Watcom stack-probe prologue (push 0x38; call FUN_0004361a) then sub esp,0x28; builds a 0x1a-byte DOS device request header (cmd 3 = IOCTL input, transfer address from [0x69da8], count 0x0b) plus an 11-byte MSCDEX IOCTL-input control block whose code byte is 0x0e (Read UPC / media catalog number) with the control/ADR byte preset to 2; copies both into the DOS real-mode buffers at [0x69de8]/[0x69da4], issues the driver request via call 0003bb7d, copies the control block and the request header back, stores the request status word to [0x69e20], and if the returned control/ADR byte came back 0 (disc carries no catalog number) memsets the 7-byte UPC field to zero before copying it to the global [0x69e0f]; returns eax=1
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003bfa5 [PLATE]
Reads the CD-ROM table-of-contents summary via the MSCDEX device driver.

Algorithm:
Builds a 26-byte (0x1a) CD-ROM device driver request header on the stack: header length 0x1a, subunit 0, command code 3 (IOCTL Input), media descriptor 0, transfer address taken from the real-mode buffer pointer at 0x00069da8, transfer byte count 7, starting sector 0, volume ID pointer NULL. Next it zero-fills a 7-byte IOCTL control block and sets its first byte to 0x0A (Read Disk Info). Both blocks are copied down into the DOS-addressable low-memory staging buffers whose linear addresses are held in 0x00069de8 (request header) and 0x00069da4 (IOCTL control block), then the driver is invoked through the request dispatcher at 0003bb7d. Afterwards both blocks are copied back up from low memory and the reply is distributed: the 6 payload bytes are mirrored to 0x00069e16, the lowest track number to 0x00069e06, the highest track number to 0x00069e07, the 4-byte lead-out address is split into its three MSF components at 0x00069e08/09/0a by 0003bc3f and converted to a single frame/LBA count at 0x00069e0b by 0003bc78, and the driver status word from offset 3 of the request header is stored to 0x00069e20.

Parameters:
None. All input and output travel through file-scope globals and the low-memory staging buffers pointed to by 0x00069da4, 0x00069da8 and 0x00069de8.

Returns:
Nothing. Results are observed through the globals listed above; 0x00069e20 holds the raw device driver status word for error checking by the caller.

=== 0003c1a1 [PLATE]
push 0x14 + call 0x0004361a stack-probe prologue, then push ebx/sub esp,8; calls 0003bfa5 and 0003c0c8 (prev fn), reads globals 00069dff/00069e01/00069e07/00069e0b, returns a subtraction of two frame counts
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c217 [PLATE]
push 0x14 + stack probe, push ebx/esi/edi; calls 0003c1a1 then LEA eax-150 and successive DIV by 0x4b (75) and 0x3c (60), storing three bytes via pointer args - LBA-to-MSF conversion
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c27c [PLATE]
push 0x10 + stack probe, push ebx/esi/edi; calls 0003bfa5, reads globals 00069e07/00069e0b, subtracts 0x96 (150) and divides by 75/60, writes three bytes through pointer args
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c2e8 [PLATE]
push 0x10 + stack probe, push ebx/esi/sub esp,4; takes a frame count arg, subtracts 0x96 (150), divides by 75 then 60, writes three bytes through pointer args
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c3fa [PLATE]
one whole function: builds a 24-byte MSCDEX device request header with command 0x83 (Seek), start sector from arg1, copies it to the DOS transfer buffer at [0x00069de8], calls fdps_cd_device_request, then tail-jumps to the shared CD epilogue FUN_0003bea0 with size 0x18
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c4a7 [PLATE]
Stop CD audio playback.

Algorithm: Reserves a 16-byte local scratch area and fills the first three bytes with a DOS CD-ROM device driver request header: length = 0x0D (13 bytes), subunit = 0, command code = 0x85 (Stop Audio Play). The header is copied into the real-mode-addressable request buffer pointed to by DAT_00069de8 with a 13-byte memcpy, the driver request dispatcher at 0003bb7d is invoked with no further arguments, then the same 13 bytes are copied back from the buffer into the local scratch area. The status word at header offset 3 is stored into the global DAT_00069e20, which holds the result of the most recent CD driver request.

Parameters: none.

Returns: nothing. The driver status word is left in the global at 00069e20.

=== 0003c4ff [PLATE]
PUSH 0x20 / CALL __STK stack-probe prologue, SUB ESP,0x10, builds a 3-byte DOS device request header (len=0x0d, subunit=0, command=0x88 = MSCDEX Resume Audio Play), then JMP 0003c4c2 into the shared tail of fdps_cd_stop_audio
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c51c [PLATE]
whole function, 0003c51c-0003c56a: PUSH 0x2c / CALL __STK prologue, SUB ESP,0x1c, copies the caller's first stack argument (byte at [ESP+0x20]) into the IOCTL data byte at frame+0x18, builds a 24-byte IOCTL-Output request header (len=0x18, subunit=0, command=0x0c, media=0, transfer addr = real-mode far pointer from [0x00069da8], count=1), memcpy's the header into the DOS-addressable request buffer [0x00069de8], then PUSH 0x1 and falls through into the shared CD request tail at 0003c56b (the tail-call JMP is elided because the target is adjacent)
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c5a6 [PLATE]
single function, whole block: PUSH 0x2c / CALL 0004361a (__STK stack probe) prologue, SUB ESP,0x1c local frame; takes one stack argument at [ESP+0x20], a pointer to an 11-byte MSCDEX control block, and writes code byte 0x0c (Audio Q-Channel Info) into its first byte; builds a 26-byte DOS device driver request header on the stack (len 0x1a, subunit 0, command 3 = IOCTL input, media 0, transfer address from [0x00069da8], byte count 6); memcpy's the header into the DOS buffer [0x00069de8] and the caller's control block into the DOS buffer [0x00069da4] via FUN_000435bc = memcpy(dst,src,n); calls fdps_cd_device_request (0003bb7d); memcpy's the header back out; then PUSH 0xb / JMP 0003be27 to enter the shared copy-back epilogue
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c636 [PLATE]
CD-ROM presence check, called once from main during startup. main sign-extends the
result and aborts with "Fatal error: CDROM is not install!!!" unless it is exactly 1.

Algorithm:
  1. PUSH 0x10 / CALL __CHK (0004361a) - Watcom stack probe for the 16 bytes this call
     chain needs. Not a parameter.
  2. Fills the input union REGS at 00069dc8: AL=0 (00069dc8), AH=0x15 (00069dc9),
     BX=0 (00069dcc), i.e. AX=1500h - the MSCDEX installation check.
  3. int386(0x2f, &in_regs at 00069dc8, &out_regs at 00069dac) via 00043268.
     On return the output block holds BX at 00069db0 (number of CD-ROM drive letters)
     and CX at 00069db4 (index of the first CD-ROM drive letter, 0 = A:).
  4. If BX == 0 no CD-ROM redirector is loaded: return 0 immediately.
  5. Otherwise publishes the result to the CD subsystem globals: the drive count word
     goes to 00069dfc and the low byte of the first-drive-letter index to 00069dfe.
  6. If the flag at 00069e54 is still zero, calls the one-time CD initialiser at
     0003bade, then always calls 0003bfa5, which leaves a 16-bit status word at
     00069e20.
  7. Returns 2 if that status word is 0x810C, otherwise 1.

Parameters:
  None. The decompiler's param_1/param_2/unaff_EBX are artifacts: param_1 is the
  0x10 stack-probe immediate, and nothing in the body reads an incoming register or
  a stack slot.

Returns:
  0 - no MSCDEX / CD-ROM redirector installed (INT 2Fh AX=1500h returned BX=0).
  1 - CD-ROM found and the device request completed with a status other than 0x810C.
  2 - CD-ROM found but the device request left status 0x810C at 00069e20.
  Only 1 is accepted by main; both 0 and 2 end the program.

=== 0003c6bc [PLATE]
Function entry: Watcom stack-check prologue (push 8; call __STK at 0004361a), then cdecl call to the MSCDEX IOCTL-Output helper 0003c51c with control code 5 (Close Tray) and add esp,4; falls through into the tail-merged status block at 0003c6d0, so the function really spans 0003c6bc-0003c6e7 and returns via the RET at 0003c6e7
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003c6e8 [PLATE]
Query whether the CD-ROM drive has finished the current audio playback.

Algorithm:
  1. Watcom stack-overflow probe: PUSH 4 / CALL __STK at 0004361a (compiler
     prologue, not a real argument to this function).
  2. Calls the CD request builder at 0003c34f, which fills a DOS device driver
     request header and submits it via fdps_cd_device_request (0003bb7d).
  3. Tail-jumps into the shared epilogue at 0003c6d0, which loads the 16-bit
     request header status word at 0069e20, clears the low byte (error code
     bits), masks AH with 0x02 (status bit 9 = BUSY) and returns SETZ of the
     result. So the value is 1 when the drive is no longer busy and 0 while it
     is still busy.
  The neighbouring function at 0003c6bc falls through into the same epilogue
  after issuing a different request; 0003c6d0 is a shared tail, not a callee in
  the source.

Parameters:
  none. The pushed 4 belongs to the Watcom stack-check helper.

Returns:
  16-bit flag in AX: 1 when the request header BUSY bit (0x0200) is clear
  (audio idle / command complete), 0 while the drive reports busy.

=== 0003c85b [PLATE]
Starts CD-DA playback of a single track.

Algorithm:
1. Calls the argument-forwarding thunk at 0004361a with the constant 0x0c (the thunk swaps the pushed value into EAX, calls 0004362d and returns popping its argument, so ESP is unchanged on return).
2. Calls fdps_cd_stop_audio to halt any track currently playing.
3. Sign-extends the 16-bit track argument at [ESP+4] and passes it to FUN_0003c0c8, which selects/queries the requested track (caller cleans the 4-byte argument).
4. Calls FUN_0003c803, the only writer of the globals at 00069dec and 00069de4; it derives the playback start and end positions for the selected track (00069de4 is written on two paths, i.e. the end position is conditional).
5. Pushes 00069de4 then 00069dec and calls FUN_0003c452, so that routine receives (value_at_00069dec, value_at_00069de4) - the start and end positions - and performs the actual play request. The 8 bytes of arguments are cleaned by this function.

Parameters:
  track - 16-bit track index, passed on the stack and read with MOVSX from [ESP+4].

Returns:
  Nothing. EAX is not set deliberately; the value left in it comes from the last call.

=== 0003c892 [PLATE]
push 0x14 / call 0x4361a __STK stack-check prologue; two 16-bit args passed on stack, calls 0003c4a7, 0003c0c8, 0003c803 twice, then returns dword [0x69de4] - dword [0x69dec] in EAX
Pool hint from block triage: fdps

=== 0003d375 [PLATE]
Watcom C library malloc.

Algorithm: Loads the caller-pushed size from [ESP+4], re-pushes it, calls the near-heap allocator core at 0003d383 (which rounds the request up to a 4-byte multiple with a 12-byte minimum, rejects zero and sizes >= 0xFFFFFFD5, walks the heap list at DAT_00060388/DAT_0006038c/DAT_00060390, and falls back to heap expansion), then pops the argument and returns. The thunk itself performs no bookkeeping; the allocator's return value is left untouched in EAX. Because the callee also clears the out-of-memory flag DAT_00069ead, no error handling happens here.

Parameters: size - number of bytes requested, passed on the stack (cdecl); the caller removes it.

Returns: pointer to the allocated block, or NULL when the size is zero, out of range, or the heap cannot satisfy the request.

=== 0003d478 [PLATE]
Watcom C library free().

Algorithm: Loads the single stack argument from [ESP+4], pushes it and calls the internal near-heap release routine at 0003d486, then pops the argument. The internal routine walks the mini-heap segment list starting at the heap-begin pointer (DAT_00060388) until it finds the segment whose range contains the pointer, hands the block plus the owning segment to the free-list insert helper (FUN_00043d94), updates the largest-free-block-before-rover cache (DAT_00060390) when the segment is not the rover segment (DAT_0006038c), and clears the heap-state flag at DAT_00069ead. This wrapper adds nothing beyond the stack-to-stack call, which is the usual free()/__nfree() split in the Watcom runtime.

Parameters: void *ptr - pointer previously returned by the matching allocator; behaviour for a pointer outside every mini-heap segment is whatever the internal routine does, no validation happens here.

Returns: nothing.

=== 0003d8b2 [PLATE]
Miles Sound System AIL API entry point: AIL_shutdown().

Algorithm:
The function is the standard Miles API wrapper generated for every public AIL
entry point. It increments the global API nesting depth counter at 0x00069e6c
on entry and decrements it on exit. If the debug log is enabled (0x00069e80
non-zero) and either this is the outermost API call (depth == 1) or nested
logging is enabled (0x00069e84 non-zero), and the "inside interrupt/DOS-unsafe"
guard at 0x00044f42 returns zero, and the log-line prologue helper at 0x0003d676
succeeds, then the literal text "AIL_shutdown()\n" at 0x000623a5 is written to
the log file handle held in 0x00069e7c via the printf-style writer at 0x00045183
(cdecl, two stack arguments cleaned by the caller).
The real work is then performed by the internal shutdown routine at 0x00045af9,
which takes no arguments. Afterwards the same log-enable test is repeated and,
if it passes, the log stream at 0x00069e7c is flushed through 0x000428be.
The function has no early exits and always falls through to the depth decrement.

Parameters:
None.

Returns:
Nothing.

=== 00042e0f [PLATE]
C runtime exit().

Algorithm:
  Calls two runtime finalizer hooks indirectly through the function-pointer
  slots at 0x000603a0 and 0x000603a4, then pushes its own status argument and
  calls the next termination stage at 0x00042e29 (_exit), cleaning the argument
  off the stack afterwards.

  The three adjacent slots 0x000603a0 / 0x000603a4 / 0x000603a8 form the
  runtime's finalizer hook table. exit() runs slots [0] and [1]; the _exit
  stage at 0x00042e29 runs slots [1] and [2] and then jumps into the cstart
  termination tail at 0x00043527, which pops the status into EAX and reaches
  INT 21h AH=4Ch. In this build all three slots are statically initialised to
  0x00042e0e, a bare RET stub, i.e. no finalizer module was linked in, so the
  hooks are effectively no-ops.

  The trailing RET is unreachable: control never comes back from 0x00042e29.
  This function does not return.

Parameters:
  [ESP+4]  int status - process exit code, forwarded unchanged to _exit and
           ultimately to the DOS terminate call.

Returns:
  Does not return.

=== 00042fd9 [EOL]

=== 00043296 [EOL]
2-byte 00 00 alignment filler between FUN_00043268 and crt_cstart

=== 00043298 [PLATE]
LE module entry point (Watcom cstart_.asm).

Algorithm:
  The whole body is one 2-byte instruction, JMP 0x00043310 (EB 76). The 0x76
  bytes it jumps over are data, not code: the ASCII banner "WATCOM C/C++32
  Run-Time system. (c) Copyright by WATCOM International Corp. 1988-1994. All
  rights reserved." at 0004329A, a 3-byte LEA EAX,[EAX+0] pad, the runtime
  version/flag words 0003 0001, and the default console device name "con" at
  0004330C. Control lands on the startup body at 00043310, which begins
  STI / AND ESP,-4 / MOV EBX,ESP, identifies the DOS extender (RSX 'DX',
  'CB', or DPMI via INT 21h AX=FF00 'PHAR'), shrinks the DOS memory block,
  records PSP/environment pointers, copies the command tail, scans the
  environment for the "no87=" variable, clears BSS, runs the initialiser
  chain and finally exits through INT 21h AH=4Ch.

  This stub exists only so the entry address can sit immediately before the
  banner that the linker embeds; it is emitted by the Watcom startup object
  linked in by wlink and has no counterpart in hand-written C.

Parameters:
  None. Execution arrives here from the LE header's initial CS:EIP, set up by
  the DOS/4G loader; nothing is passed in registers or on the stack by any
  caller inside the image. Ghidra's six-parameter signature is an artifact of
  the decompiler inlining the jump target.

Returns:
  Does not return. The startup path it enters terminates the process with
  INT 21h AH=4Ch.

=== 0004329a [EOL]
The jump-over region inside Watcom's _cstart_ (cstrt386.obj, CLIB3S 10.0a): copyright banner, align-4 nop filler, a relocated pointer to ___begtext, and the "con" device name used by __do_exit_with_msg__ at 0004352a

=== 00043305 [EOL]
8d 40 00 alignment filler (lea eax,[eax+0x0] nop form) padding the banner up to the 4-byte boundary at 00043308

=== 00043308 [EOL]
0x00010003 = &___begtext, a relocated 32-bit code pointer to BEGTEXT+3, i.e. the first NOP after the INT3/JMP-self trap stub at 00010000; emitted by cstrt386.obj itself, read by nothing in the linked image

=== 0004330c [EOL]
"con\0", DOS device name loaded into EDX at 0004352c before INT 21h AX=3D01 (open for write)

=== 00043310 [PLATE]
Named crt_cstart_body rather than crt_cstart because the LE entry symbol at
00043298 owns that name; the two are one piece of assembly separated only by
the banner data the linker embeds between them.

Watcom C/C++ 10.0a runtime startup code (cstrt386.asm, _cstart_). This is the
real program entry: the LE entry stub at 00043298 is a bare JMP to here. Hand
written assembly, not compiled C.

Algorithm:
  1. STI, align ESP down to a dword boundary, and record the initial stack
     pointer in the runtime globals at 00060434 and 00060420. Preload the
     default selector 0x24 into 0006042c and EBX with 'PHAR' for the Phar Lap
     probe.
  2. INT 21h AH=30h (get DOS version). AL/AH are stored as the DOS major and
     minor version at 00060457 and 00060458. The high half of EAX selects the
     host:
       - 'DX'  (0x4458): Phar Lap 386|DOS-Extender. Take the version digit from
         BL, read the PSP memory-block end at ES:[5Ch] via selector 0006042c,
         round it up to a 4K boundary, store it at 00060420 and shrink the
         block with INT 21h AH=4Ah. Environment selector 0x2C.
       - 'CB'  (0x4243): Intel Code Builder. EDX points at the Code Builder
         info block; it is saved at 000603ac. Shrink memory by the amount at
         [EDX+1Ch] (INT 21h AH=4Ah), take the PSP pointer from [EDX+10h] (the
         command tail therefore starts at PSP+81h) and the environment segment
         from [PSP+2Ch], shifted left by 4.
       - otherwise: INT 21h AX=FF00h with DX=78h. If it reports a host, this is
         a DPMI/DOS-4G style extender: the GS selector, when non-zero, is saved
         at 000603b0, INT 31h AX=0006h fetches the DS segment base, the PSP
         selector is taken from ES and the environment selector from ES:[2Ch].
         If it reports nothing, fall back to the fixed real-mode-ish selectors
         (PSP 0x24, environment 0x2C, flat data 0x17).
     The resulting host type/subtype byte pair is written to 0006044e/0006044f,
     the environment pointer to 00060451 and its selector to 00060455.
  3. Self-modifying step: with the flat data selector in ES, store DS into the
     code at 0004356d, which is the operand of the "MOV DS,CS:[4356Dh]"
     instruction in the exit path at 00043564.
  4. Command line: read the tail length from PSP:80h, skip leading spaces with
     REPE SCASB, and copy the remaining tail with REP MOVSB to the 16-byte
     aligned buffer just past the end of BSS (0006a3bc rounded up), then append
     two NUL bytes. The buffer address is kept in 00060428.
  5. Environment scan: walk the environment strings; a string whose first four
     bytes lowercase to "no87" and whose fifth byte is '=' increments the
     no-x87 flag stored at 0006044c. After the terminating double NUL, skip the
     word count and copy the program name that follows to the end of the
     command line buffer; the pointer past it is stored at 00060430. The stack
     pointer is also recorded at 0006041c.
  6. Zero BSS from 00063930 up to 0006a3bc with REP STOSD plus a byte tail.
     When the host type byte at 0006044e is 1 (the DPMI path, where the loader
     already zero-fills the image) the cleared span is clamped to 0x1000 bytes.
     The aligned end of BSS is then stored at 00060424 as the heap/break base.
  7. Call __InitRtns (0004dfa6) with EAX=0FFh to run every registered
     initializer up to priority 255, clear EBP, then call __CMain (0004df4c),
     which sets up the stack limit, builds argc/argv and calls main (00029220),
     then exit (00042e0f). __CMain therefore never returns; the POP EAX / JMP
     00043528 tail that follows it only executes on paths that reach the shared
     exit sequence at 0004354d, which runs __FiniRtns (0004dff1) with
     EAX=0, EDX=0FFh and terminates with INT 21h AH=4Ch.

Parameters: none at the C level. The only incoming register state that is read
is supplied by the extender/loader: EDX (Intel Code Builder info block) and GS
(DPMI). The incoming stack contents are not used; ESP is only realigned and
recorded.

Returns: never returns. Control leaves through main/exit inside __CMain, or
through the INT 21h AH=4Ch terminate in the shared exit tail at 0004354d.

=== 0004356f [EOL]
single 00 alignment byte before FUN_00043570

=== 000435a2 [PLATE]
Watcom CRT variadic formatting entry point (sprintf).

Algorithm:
Preserves EBX, then rebuilds a three-argument stack frame for the internal
worker at 0004e1e9: it computes LEA EAX,[ESP+0x10], which is the address of
the first variadic slot on the caller's stack (a va_list), and pushes it last
of the three so the worker receives (dest, format, va_list) in cdecl order.
The first two incoming stack arguments are re-pushed unchanged. After the
call the wrapper removes the 12 bytes it pushed (ADD ESP,0xc), restores EBX
and returns; EAX is left untouched, so the worker's return value is the
return value of this function. 0004e1e9 in turn appends a global pointer read
from 00061454 and calls the formatting engine at 00054dc7. All three
functions in that chain have exactly one caller each, and the only caller of
this wrapper is fdps_play_movie at 00030f40, so the whole formatting chain was
linked in for a single call site.

Parameters:
  arg1 (stack, [ESP+8] at entry) - destination buffer receiving the formatted
    text.
  arg2 (stack, [ESP+0xc] at entry) - format string.
  ... (stack, from [ESP+0x10] at entry) - variadic arguments consumed by the
    conversion specifiers; their address is passed on as a va_list.

Returns:
  int, propagated unchanged from the worker (character count in the CRT
  contract).

=== 00043c82 [PLATE]
Watcom C runtime initialiser: calibrate the delay() busy-wait loop.

Algorithm:
  Phase 1 (sync): issue DOS "Get System Time" (INT 21h, AH=2Ch; returns
  CH=hour, CL=minute, DH=second, DL=hundredths), keep the seconds field
  (DH), then re-issue the call until DH changes. This aligns the
  measurement with a whole-second boundary so no partial second is
  counted.
  Phase 2 (count): with the new seconds value latched in BL, spin issuing
  INT 21h AH=2Ch and incrementing a counter until DH changes again. The
  counter therefore holds the number of INT 21h AH=2Ch round trips this
  machine performs in one second.
  The result is stored to the global at 0x00069ea8. delay() at 0x00043cb0
  is its only consumer: it computes
  iterations = (milliseconds * [0x00069ea8] + 500) / 1000, clamps a zero
  result up to 1, and spins that many INT 21h AH=2Ch calls. The stored
  value is thus "loop iterations per 1000 ms" on the host CPU.

  The routine is not reached by a CALL. It is entry 0 of the Watcom
  runtime init table at 0x000638f0 (6-byte records: status byte,
  priority byte, 32-bit routine address); its record reads
  00 20 82 3C 04 00 - status 0 (pending), priority 0x20
  (INIT_PRIORITY_LIBRARY), routine 0x00043C82. The table walker at
  0x0004DFA6 invokes it during startup and then marks the record 2 (done).

Parameters:
  None. EBX and ESI are pushed on entry and popped on exit; every other
  register the body reads (EAX, EDX) is written by INT 21h before being
  used, so no incoming register or stack argument exists.

Returns:
  void. The calibration result is delivered through the global at
  0x00069ea8. Stack is balanced by a plain RET (no purge).

EBP is a live inbound register holding the NO87 environment flag set up by crt_cstart_body, not an uninitialised local. Ends with fninit + fldcw [0x00061448] + 4x fldz, pops, ret
Pool hint from block triage: crt

=== 0004df4c [PLATE]
Watcom C/C++ 10.0a CRT routine __CMain, from library module cmain386 (CLIB3S.LIB).
Byte-identical to the library copy; the externs it references are, in order,
__ASTACKSIZ, stackavail, __ASTACKPTR, __CommonInit, ___Argv, ___Argc, main, exit.

Algorithm:
  1. Load __ASTACKSIZ (0x00060438), round it up to a multiple of 4 ((n + 3) & ~3,
     coded as ADD EBX,3 / AND BL,0FCh).
  2. Call stackavail (0x0004e692, ESP - __STACKLOW at 0x00060430). If the rounded
     size is smaller than the free stack, carve the block off the stack with
     SUB ESP,size and keep the resulting ESP as the block base; otherwise use a
     null base, i.e. no auto-stack is reserved.
  3. Store base + __ASTACKSIZ (the unrounded size) into __ASTACKPTR (0x0006043c).
     In this build __ASTACKSIZ is 0 and __ASTACKPTR is never read back, so the
     whole step is inert here.
  4. Call __CommonInit (0x00054dbc), which sets _amblksiz (0x000613c4) to 0x8000.
  5. Push _argv (0x0006a3b8) then _argc (0x0006a3b4), call main (0x00029220),
     clean the two arguments off the stack, then push main's return value and
     call exit (0x00042e0f).

Control never comes back: exit runs the atexit/fini chains and terminates. The
epilogue (MOV ESP,EBP / POP EBP / POP EBX / RET) exists only to restore the frame
and the saved EBX and is unreachable in practice.

The single caller is the DOS/4G CRT startup at 0x00043310, which invokes
__InitRtns(255) at 0x0004dfa6, then this function, then __FiniRtns(0,255) at
0x0004dff1 before the INT 21h/AH=4Ch fallback.

Parameters: none. EBX and EBP are callee-saved here, not inputs; every other
register is written before it is read, and RET performs no stack purge.

Returns: nothing (void). The function does not return to its caller.

=== 0004df9d [EOL]
Unreachable epilogue tail of crt_cmain (0004df4c) at 0004df9d-0004dfa4; extend that function's body to 0004dfa4, do not create a new function here. The final byte 0004dfa5 is not part of the epilogue: it is a one-byte alignment pad, judged separately in verdicts/blocks/0004dfa5.json (kind pad, pool binary_artifact).

=== 0004dfa5 [EOL]
Single 0x00 alignment byte between crt_cmain and crt_init_rtns.

=== 0004dfa6 [PLATE]
Watcom C runtime __InitRtns -- run the link-time initialiser table.

Algorithm:
The linker gathers records of the form

    struct rt_init { unsigned char rtn_type;      /* 2 == already run */
                     unsigned char priority;
                     void (*rtn)( void ); };      /* 6 bytes each */

into a contiguous "XI" table. Here that table spans 0x000638f0
(inclusive) to 0x00063920 (exclusive), i.e. 8 entries; a matching
"YI" rundown table begins at 0x00063920 and is walked by the
termination routine at 0x0004dff1.

Outer loop, repeated until no candidate remains:
  - set the running minimum priority to the caller-supplied bound and
    the selected entry to the table end (the "nothing found" sentinel);
  - scan the whole table forward; an entry is a candidate when its
    rtn_type is not 2 (not yet executed) and its priority is <= the
    running minimum, in which case it becomes the new selection and
    lowers the minimum. Because the test is <= rather than <, a later
    entry with a priority equal to the current minimum displaces an
    earlier one, so entries sharing a priority execute in reverse
    table order;
  - if nothing was selected, return;
  - otherwise reload ES from DS, call the entry's function pointer
    when it is non-NULL, and stamp rtn_type = 2 so the entry is never
    run twice.

The current table holds eight entries, all rtn_type 0, with priorities
0x01 (0x0003d50a), 0x02 (0x00044749) and six at 0x20 (0x00043c82,
0x0004cfc8, 0x0004e040, 0x00043612, 0x00054893, 0x00054f86).

Parameters:
  EAX (only AL is used) -- the priority ceiling. Entries whose
  priority exceeds it are skipped. The startup code at 0x00043310
  passes 0xff, i.e. "run everything".

Returns:
  Nothing. ESI, EDI, EBX, EDX and the ES segment register are saved on
  entry and restored before returning.

=== 0004e040 [PLATE]
Watcom prologue 53 56 57 55 83 ec 08; builds the argv vector: parses [0x00060424] cmdline twice via 0004e104 (count pass, then fill pass), mallocs via 0003d375, rep-movs the cmdline into it, stores argv[0] from [0x00060428] and publishes argc/argv to 0x000603b4/0x000603b8 and 0x0006a3b4/0x0006a3b8
Pool hint from block triage: crt

=== 0004e692 [PLATE]
Watcom C runtime: stackavail() -- report remaining stack space.

Algorithm:
  Copies ESP into EAX and subtracts the global stack-low limit stored at
  0x00060430, then returns. Because ESP at function entry already points at
  this call's own return address, the reported figure is 4 bytes smaller than
  the space the caller actually has, which is conservative and harmless.
  The same 0x00060430 global is the limit used by the compiler-generated
  stack-overflow probe at 0x0004362d (which tests ESP-size against it) and is
  initialised by the startup code at 0x000434c0, confirming it is __STACKLOW
  rather than a stack base or margin constant.

  All three callers are CRT internals that use the result to choose between an
  on-stack scratch buffer and the heap: 0x0004df4c and 0x000556a9 both compare
  (size + 3) & ~3 against the return value before pointing their buffer at a
  local array or at NULL, and 0x00043ab4 (the DOS int 21h AH=40h write path
  with LF->CRLF translation) calls the stack-overflow handler at 0x0004364b
  when fewer than 0xB0 bytes remain and otherwise picks a 0x200-byte staging
  buffer when at least 0x230 bytes are free, falling back to 0x80 bytes.

Parameters:
  None. EAX is written before any register is read, and no argument register
  or stack slot is consumed.

Returns:
  EAX = bytes of stack remaining below the current stack pointer, as an
  unsigned value. Every caller treats it as unsigned.

Pool hint from block triage: crt

=== 000508ba [PLATE]
5-byte selector stub: PUSH EBX / MOV BL,0xa0 / JMP 0x00050917. One of three stubs feeding the shared conversion body at 0x00050917; the other two are 0x0005090f (BL=0x1f) and 0x00050914 (BL=0x20). BL is not an emulated-opcode selector: bits 0..5 are the maximum permitted integer width in bits (checked as AND BL,0x3f / CMP CL,BL at 00050935) and bit 7 enables round-to-nearest instead of truncation, so 0x1f = 31 bits truncating (signed 32-bit destination), 0x20 = 32 bits truncating (unsigned 32-bit destination) and 0xa0 = 32 bits with rounding. === 000518d0 [PLATE]
extended-precision square root, in place on the 10-byte long double pointed to by EAX (dword[0] low mantissa, dword[4] high mantissa, word[8] sign/exponent). Special cases first: mantissa zero and exponent zero returns the value untouched (+-0), raw exponent word exactly 0x7fff returns untouched (+inf), sign-stripped exponent 0x7fff (NaN or -inf), sign set (negative operand) or integer bit clear (unnormal) all fall into OR byte[EAX+7],0xc0 with exponent 0xffff, i.e. real indefinite. Normal path unbiases the exponent (SUB CX,0x3fff), halves it (SAR CX,1) and, when the exponent was even, shifts the 64-bit mantissa right one bit into a 96-bit EDX:EBX:EDI, rebiases and stores the new exponent; the mantissa root is then computed by a Newton-Raphson loop x=(x+n/x)/2 built from 64/32 DIV plus STC/RCR, and the 64-bit result is written back through the pointer saved on the stack. Saves EDI/ESI/EDX/ECX/EBX, ends RET at 0005199b
Pool hint from block triage: crt

=== 00054dbc [PLATE]
Watcom CRT __CommonInit, identified by position in __CMain's extern order
(__ASTACKSIZ, stackavail, __ASTACKPTR, __CommonInit, ___Argv, ___Argc, main,
exit) rather than by its body, which shows only the _amblksiz store below.

Watcom C runtime helper: install the default heap growth granularity.

Algorithm:
  Stores the constant 0x8000 (32768) into the global at 0x000613c4 and returns.
  That global is the Watcom CRT variable `_amblksiz`, the minimum amount by
  which the heap manager grows the heap when it has to ask the system for more
  memory. Identification of the global rests on two other users of it:
    - 0x0004442b (heap grow helper) clamps a requested growth amount up to
      `_amblksiz` (with the low bit masked off, the documented Watcom flag bit)
      and then rounds the result up to a 4 KiB page boundary.
    - 0x0005600f (Watcom __cenvarg, the DOS spawn/exec command line and
      environment block builder) saves the global, forces it to 16 while it
      allocates the tightly sized argument block, and restores it afterwards -
      the exact save/set-16/restore idiom of the library source.
  The image initialises the global to 16; this routine raises it to 32 KiB so
  that a DOS extender heap is grown in large chunks instead of many small
  extender calls. It is called from the CRT startup path (0x0004df4c, which
  sizes the stack, then calls main at 0x00029220 and passes its result to exit
  at 0x00042e0f), so it runs exactly once before user code starts.

Parameters:
  None. The function reads no register and no stack slot.

Returns:
  Nothing. Only the global at 0x000613c4 is modified; no register is written.

Interior gap of FUN_00054f26: WVIDEO INT3 API signature plus the success return path (mov eax,1; add esp,8; ret)

=== 00054f3f [EOL]

=== 000567f0 [PLATE]
Installs the game's keyboard (INT 09h / IRQ1) interrupt handler.

Algorithm:
  1. Saves DS and ES.
  2. INT 21h with EAX=0x3509 (DOS Get Interrupt Vector, vector 09h). Under
     DOS/4G this returns the protected-mode vector in ES:EBX.
  3. Stores the returned handler offset (EBX) at 0x00070002 and the returned
     selector (ES) at 0x00070000, so the old vector can be restored later.
  4. Loads DS from CS so DS:EDX addresses code in this module, points EDX at
     the handler 0x00056837, and issues INT 21h with AH=0x25, AL=0x09 (DOS Set
     Interrupt Vector) to hook IRQ1.
  5. Restores ES and DS and returns.

Hand-written assembly: it manipulates segment registers directly and passes
arguments to DOS in explicit registers, which the C compiler would not emit.

Parameters: none.
Returns: nothing.

=== 00056837 [PLATE]
Keyboard IRQ1 interrupt service routine (protected mode, DOS/4G).

Installed by fdps_install_keyboard_isr at 0005680a; it is never called
directly, only entered through the interrupt vector, and exits with IRETD.

Algorithm:
  1. STI, then save EDX/ECX/EBX/EAX and DS on the interrupt stack, and load
     DS with selector 0x0002 so the handler can reach the program data
     segment regardless of the interrupted context.
  2. IN AL,0x60 reads the raw scancode; it is stored unconditionally in the
     last-scancode byte at 0x00070006.
  3. IN AL,0x61, set bit 7, OUT it back, then clear bit 7 and OUT again --
     the classic XT-style keyboard acknowledge strobe on port 0x61.
  4. Compare the new scancode with the previous one at 0x00070021. If they
     are equal, nothing is queued (this suppresses the auto-repeat stream and
     duplicate reports). Otherwise the new value becomes the previous one.
  5. If the scancode is below 0x80 (a make code, i.e. key press rather than
     the 0x80-or-above break code), store it into the byte ring buffer that
     begins at 0x0007000f, indexed by the write cursor at 0x0007001d, then
     increment the cursor and wrap it to 0 when it reaches 10. The buffer has
     10 entries and the cursor is a pure write index -- the consumer side
     tracks its own read position.
  6. OUT 0x20,0x20 sends the non-specific EOI to the master PIC.
  7. Restore DS and the four saved registers and return with IRETD.

Note that break codes and repeats only update the last-scancode bytes; they
never enter the ring buffer. The original interrupt handler is not chained.

Parameters:
  None. Entered from the interrupt gate, not by a call, so no arguments are
  passed in registers or on the stack. The decompiler's invented param_1 is
  the interrupt frame and is not real.

Returns:
  Nothing. Exits with IRETD; all side effects are in the globals at
  0x00070006 (last scancode), 0x00070021 (previous scancode for the repeat
  filter), 0x0007000f (10-byte scancode ring buffer) and 0x0007001d (ring
  write index).

=== 000568b7 [PLATE]
Algorithm: Runs a self-inverse XOR stream cipher over a byte buffer in place. A 16-bit key register is seeded with 0xA5. For each of len bytes: key = rol16(key + 0x9014, 3), then the source byte is XORed with the low 8 bits of key and stored back at the same offset (the assembly uses LODSB/STOSB with ESI and EDI both initialised from buf, so it is strictly in place). The key stream depends only on the byte index, never on the data, so the same call both encrypts and decrypts. The loop is driven by LOOP with the count in ECX, so a length of 0 wraps and processes 0x100000000 bytes; callers must pass a positive length. Written as hand-tuned assembly with stack-passed arguments rather than the Watcom register convention used elsewhere in the module.

Parameters: buf - pointer to the buffer to transform in place; len - number of bytes to process, must be greater than zero.

Returns: nothing; the buffer is modified in place.

=== 000568db [PLATE]
Blit mode dispatcher: publishes the blit geometry into the shared graphics globals and jumps into the inner copy loop chosen by the mode byte.

Algorithm:
  Loads the source pointer into ESI and the destination pointer into EDI, which the mode handlers consume directly as x86 string-operation registers. Stores the destination pitch (arg 5) into the word at 0x0007002e, the run width (arg 3) into the word at 0x00070024, and the row count (arg 4) into the word at 0x00070022, then computes EDX = pitch - width, the number of bytes to advance the destination pointer at the end of each row. Clears EAX and ECX and loads the mode byte (arg 7) into BH. A linear chain of compares on BH selects one of thirteen handlers for modes 0 through 12 (0x00056a0d, 0x00056a8d, 0x00056b25, 0x00056bb7, 0x00056c5e, 0x00056e2a, 0x00057114, 0x00057551, 0x000575ed, 0x0005761b, 0x00057793, 0x00057916, 0x00057a74); every branch converges on the common epilogue, and a mode byte above 12 falls through and draws nothing. The handlers are called, not tail-jumped, so the frame pointer set up here stays live for them; the sixth argument at [EBP+0x1c] is never touched by this function and is presumably read from that frame by individual handlers.

Parameters:
  src    - source pixel data, loaded into ESI
  dst    - destination surface pointer, loaded into EDI
  width  - bytes copied per row, also stored to the global at 0x00070024
  height - number of rows, also stored to the global at 0x00070022
  pitch  - destination stride, stored to the global at 0x0007002e; pitch - width is passed to the handler in EDX
  arg6   - unused by this function, left on the frame for the handlers
  mode   - blit mode selector, 0 through 12; other values are a no-op

Returns:
  Nothing. All output goes to the destination surface and the shared blit globals.
